Four reasons: the guidance names gradient washes and soft grey shadows as defaults; the window can now be see-through (D42, D45), and a gradient under a see-through ground gives every contrast figure a second unknown where a flat tone gives one; each gradient stop would be a token the Omarchy source has no value for; and the tone steps are enough: the ground and the card (which is also the preview's frame) are two steps of one family, 1.26:1 apart in the dark theme (Lagoon to Tide) and 1.29:1 in the light one (Frost to Glacier), and the controls on them are a third, the text colour at 4 or 5 per cent.

**The window's opacity (Tasks 45 and 51).**
- **How the app follows Omarchy's Window Opacity:**
  - (a) not at all, as until now: the owner's complaint;
  - (b) ship a Hyprland window rule with the package: against rule 14, and no use on any other compositor, with the own theme's setting still to build;
  - (c) **chosen:** draw it. A GTK app paints solid backgrounds, which is why Omarchy fades its one GTK app as a whole; the app paints its ground and cards with an alpha, which also keeps its text opaque.
- **Which surfaces carry the alpha:**
  - (a) the window's background alone, the cards opaque: the cards are most of the Configuration page, so a glassy window would show glass only in its margins;
  - (b) **chosen:** the ground and the cards, with the exceptions named in [Window opacity and corners](#window-opacity-and-corners): what carries the picture or stands over text stays solid.
- **The numbers:** transparent in Omarchy is 0.985 focused and 0.96 unfocused. (a) both, through `:backdrop`: twice the tokens and rules for 2.5 points no one can see; (b) **chosen:** 0.985 alone, since the window being used is the focused one. Glassy: 0.75 is what Omarchy gives Nautilus, the one GTK app it makes see-through, and 0.65 is a terminal's own background alpha, which keeps a terminal's text crisp by painting one flat background. **Chosen:** 0.75, the number of the same kind of app, which also keeps more headroom for the contrast of what is drawn on it.
- **The corners:** (a) the window alone, the owner's word "window borders": a sharp window of rounded cards; (b) the window, the boxes and the controls, the plan's reading, leaving rounded switches in square troughs of cards; (c) **chosen:** everything the app draws with a curve of its own, and only the dots, which are what the product draws, stay round.

**The Default Theme subcategory (Task 50).** **Superseded in part on 2026-11-24 (Task 63, D55):** the structure below stands, but the "dimmed" look it names, `--muted` text with the saved value's button kept readable by `--fg`, is history: the section is disabled in the one disabled look of the app, and what the dimmed Window Opacity and Window Corners show is now a frame in `--disabled` ([Answers decided on 2026-11-24, Task 63](#answers-decided-on-2026-11-24-task-63)). The reasoning against (b) below, that "dimming is a muted colour", was the cause of the owner's complaint.
- **The heading's look:**
  - (a) the card heading's own style (1 em, 600): two equal headings in one card read as two sections, and the owner asked for a child of Theme;
  - (b) 1 em, 400, `--muted`: dimming is a muted colour, so a muted heading couldn't show that it is dimmed;
  - (c) the rows indented under it: the controls would start 14 px right of the Use system theme switch, or the label blocks grow and move every control of the page, breaking the one column ([Rows](#rows));
  - (d) a tinted well around the group: padding that moves the controls the same way, and a second box in a card;
  - (e) **chosen first:** 0.83 em, weight 600, `--fg`. **Superseded by Task 55** (owner, 2026-11-23): 1 em, weight 600, `--fg`, the size of a card's own rows, with the Default Theme's rows one level down at 0.83 em. It stands 24 px under the line above it (the card's 14 and 10 more) and 14 px over its first row, which groups it with its rows by distance, as the rest of the page is grouped.
- **What the dimmed Window Opacity and Window Corners show:**
  - (a) what is in use: for Opacity, Omarchy's mode while an Omarchy theme is followed (the app reads it anyway); for Corners, nothing the app can know, since the compositor does them: half the group would show an in-use value and half would show none;
  - (b) nothing pressed: a toggle group with none pressed, which GTK's grouped toggles may not allow (**unverified**);
  - (c) **chosen:** the saved values, dimmed, the pressed one readable: each control shows the service's saved value, as every setting does (plan, Task 50). **The one exception is Appearance,** which keeps what Task 11 built and the owner has reviewed: the scheme in use, so the window never says "Light" in a dark theme. The line above the group says what is in use. If the owner finds the mixed rule odd, Appearance showing its saved value is a one-line change in the view model.

### Answers decided on 2026-11-23, Task 54

**Superseded on 2026-11-23 (Task 59, D51): history, kept for the record.** The owner rejected Meltwater a third time and gave a colour scheme of their own, which [Task 59's answers](#answers-decided-on-2026-11-23-task-59) follow. What still holds from here: the account of what the owner disliked in Coolant (a rose accent, warm cream text), which Cool Blue Forest also avoids. Every palette, name, hex and figure below is Meltwater's.

Decided under the same delegation, for the owner's list C47 to C52 of 2026-11-23: the second rejection of OpenDeepCool's own Dark and Light themes ("still terrible"), and a new pair that fits a water cooler, an ocean and ice palette in both lights. Only the eight colours of each theme changed; the token set and its names, the six control alphas, the selection alpha, the computed tokens, the radii, the type and the window's rules are Coolant's, as they stand. Nothing was run, since the designer has no shell: every contrast figure is a hand computation ([The contrast table](#the-contrast-table)), and the implementer's test is the check. The owner's words are in D46.

- **What the owner disliked in Coolant,** and so what each choice answers: the rose accent (`#fb7fb0`, `#b0145f`), the warm cream text (`#f1ece2`), and a petrol ground that, with those two, clashed with a cooling product. Wanted: ocean depth in the dark theme, ice and frost in the light one, cool and clean, one identity in two lights; an icy aqua or cyan accent in the dark theme, a deep ocean blue or teal in the light one, never pink, purple or orange; text a cool white and a deep navy ink, never warm; red, yellow and green still recognisable and retuned to the new grounds.
- **The idea:** water has two states in the cold, and the themes are those two ([the idea](#opendeepcools-own-theme)). So the two themes aren't one palette inverted: the dark theme is a deep navy sea with the glacier-cyan accent as its one light, and the light theme is the ice, a pale frost with the sea's colour as its ink and its mark.
- **The seeds,** hues and tonal steps taken from the palettes below and then set by contrast, not copied:
  - the dark ground and card from Coolors' "Deep Sea" (`0D1B2A`, `1B263B`, hues 211 and 219), "Deep Blue Sea" and "Dark Waters" (`0B132B`, `1C2541`, hue 225, too violet for a sea, so not followed) and Pikka's "Deep Navy & Glacier Cyan" (`0A192F`, hue 216) and its dark-mode tip (tint dark surfaces with a subtle blue, `#0F172A`, not black): **Abyss `#0a1f33`** at 209 degrees, a little more saturated than `0D1B2A` (67 per cent against 53) so that it reads as a colour and not as black, and **Current `#11344f`** one step up (1.30:1);
  - the dark accent from Coolors' "Ocean Blue Serenity": its cyan steps `48CAE4` and `90E0EF` share the hue 190, and **Glacier `#6bd3e8`** is their midpoint in lightness at that hue. Considered and not chosen: the mint and teal end, `6FFFE9` (171) and `5BC0BE` (179), which would sit 26 to 34 degrees from the status green and be taken for it; and the sky end, Pikka's `38BDF8` (198), the stock sky blue and the nearest to Lumon's accent (201);
  - the dark text from Pikka's `E0F2FE` and Coolors' `CAF0F8`: **Spray `#e6f3fa`** (201), a cool white; Coolors' "Deep Sea" `E0E1DD` is a warm grey and was not used. **Haze `#8db2c9`** (203) lies between Coolors' `778DA9` ("Deep Sea") and `89C2D9` ("Coastal Blues");
  - the light ground and card from Pikka's "Arctic Ice & Crisp Denim" `DBEAFE` (214) and "Pacific Ocean & White Seafoam" `F0F9FF`, and Coolors' "Ocean Blue Serenity" `CAF0F8` (190): **Rime `#cfe3f0`** (204) is darker than any of them, because a ground as pale as `DBEAFE` gives the white card only about 1.16:1, and **Floe `#f5fafd`** (203) is near-white. `DBEAFE` and `E0F2FE` as the ground were considered and not chosen for that reason;
  - the light ink from Pikka's `0A192F` and Coolors' "Coastal Blues" `012A4A` (206): **Fathom `#0b2742`** (209);
  - the light accent from Coolors' "Marine Blue" `007EA7` (195) and "Blue Lagoon" `006494` (199), and Pikka's `0284C7` (200): **Marine `#085f7d`** (195), the `007EA7` hue made deeper, because `007EA7` itself is 3.5:1 on Rime, under the 4.5 a link needs; the bluer `006494` (199) and `0C4A6E` (202) would pass, but they drift toward the stock sky hue and toward the ink's 209, away from the dark theme's 190, so the two accents would stop being one colour in two lights. **Brine `#3a5971`** (206) is the ink's muted step, near Coolors' "Deep Sea" `415A77` (212).
- **The accent's hue, and what it must not be taken for.** The accent is cyan, 190 and 195 degrees. The status green is about 44 degrees from it and the red about 186, so no status colour comes near it; where it is nearest, the dark theme's accent and green are equally light, so a status is never colour alone, as the design already holds. The accent is the ground's own hue lit, which is also what keeps the dark theme from being a navy with a stock sky-blue sticker on it: the nearest stock reference, Lumon, has a sky accent at 201 on a ground half as saturated.
- **The status colours:** the families are Coolant's, retuned. The dark theme's **Buoy `#ff665c`** (4) is Signal (`#ff6b57`, 7) moved five units in two channels, a red that is not a pink and is the cyan's opposite; **Lantern `#f6c553`** (42) is Brass (46) moved toward amber, so that on a cool ground it reads as the warm warning it is; **Sea glass `#52d68a`** (145) is Mint (142) a touch bluer. The light theme's **Lifebuoy `#bd3326`** (5, from Brick's 10) is redder and so clearer on a pale blue ground; **Ochre `#7f5300`** (39) is Bronze's amber nearly unchanged, since an amber that reaches 4.5:1 on a pale ground is this dark; and **Kelp `#1a7c4e`** (152, from Pine's 142) is bluer, a sea green, and still plainly green.
- **What the contrast table needed,** which fixed the last digits: the accent on Rime at 5.4; the warning text on Rime at 5.1; the muted text kept off the 4.5 edge on both Rime and Floe (5.6 and 7.0); the dark ground deep enough that the cyan accent gives 9.6:1 and the muted text 5.7:1 on the card; the status dots at least 3.9 on their grounds; and the alphas needing no change.
- **What is not changed and why:** the six alphas and the selection alpha (the new tones pass with them, 3.9 and 3.4 on row 12), the radii, the type, the tokens, and the rule that the accent only marks a choice. The window's opacity and corners, the see-through bounds aside ([Window opacity and corners](#window-opacity-and-corners), section 3), are untouched.
- **The mockups** change in one place, the two palette blocks of `mockups-src/app.css` (the property names and the sample Omarchy theme's block are unchanged), and are regenerated; `app.js` holds no palette colour.
- **Sources.** The palettes were fetched on 2026-11-23 and handed to the designer as their hex values; the designer did not re-fetch the pages, so their names are as given and the colours are checked here only by their hex: Coolors, "Ocean" trending palettes (coolors.co/palettes/trending/ocean: "Deep Sea" `0D1B2A 1B263B 415A77 778DA9 E0E1DD`; "Deep Blue Sea" `0B132B 1C2541 3A506B 5BC0BE 6FFFE9`; "Dark Waters" `0B132B 1C2541 3A506B 5BC0BE FFFFFF`; "Ocean Blue Serenity" `03045E … 48CAE4 90E0EF ADE8F4 CAF0F8`; "Coastal Blues" `012A4A … 2A6F97 2C7DA0 468FAF 61A5C2 89C2D9 A9D6E5`; "Blue Lagoon" `00A6FB 0582CA 006494 003554 051923`; "Marine Blue" `CCDBDC 9AD1D4 80CED7 007EA7 003249`; the second "Deep Sea" `FFFFFF 00171F 003459 007EA7 00A8E8`; "Ocean Pearl Delight" `006D77 83C5BE EDF6F9`, whose warm tones were not used) and Pikka's blog post on blue palette ideas, ocean, navy and sky (pikka.app/blog/10-blue-color-palette-ideas-ocean-navy-sky: "Deep Navy & Glacier Cyan" `0A192F 1E3A8A 38BDF8 E0F2FE`; "Pacific Ocean & White Seafoam" `0C4A6E 0284C7 38BDF8 F0F9FF`; "Arctic Ice & Crisp Denim" `1E40AF 60A5FA DBEAFE FFFFFF`; "Coastal Marine" `075985 38BDF8 E2E8F0 FDFBF7`; and its dark-mode tip). The shipped Omarchy themes were read again on 2026-11-23 (`/usr/share/omarchy/themes/*/colors.toml`, here Lumon, `#16242d`, `#8bc9eb`, `#d6e2ee`). The guidance is Anthropic's `frontend-design` skill as read on 2026-11-19 and 2026-11-22 ([above](#answers-decided-on-2026-11-19-task-11)); it was not read a third time.

### Answers decided on 2026-11-23, Task 59

Decided under the same delegation, after the owner rejected the themes a third time (2026-11-23: "the default app theme is still very bad. Please use this color scheme and create the dark and light versions") and gave a colour scheme of their own: Folk & Fey's "Cool Blue Forest", from its Winter Forest Collection. The two earlier attempts, Coolant (a rose accent on petrol) and Meltwater (a navy with a bright cyan accent), were the designer's own palettes and are not reused. This time the hues are the owner's, so the choices below are about **roles**: which swatch is the ground, the card, the text and the mark in each theme, and what each needs to be to pass [the contrast table](#the-contrast-table). Only the eight colours of each theme change, and two of the six control alphas (the normal and the hover border); the token set and its names, the other four alphas, the selection alpha, the computed tokens, the radii, the type and the window's rules are Meltwater's, as they stand. Nothing was run, since the designer has no shell: every contrast figure is a hand computation, and the implementer's test is the check. The owner's words are in D51.

**Snowday's ground, card, tiles and toggles were redone on 2026-11-24 (Task 61, D53; [the answers below](#answers-decided-on-2026-11-24-tasks-60-and-61)).** What this section says of them (Hoarfrost `#d3dce3` and Fresh snow `#f5f7f9`, the 1.29:1 step between them, the light figures of the contrast table, the Glassy bounds of the light column) is history, kept for the record, as Meltwater's is above; the new values are in [palette-winter.md](palette-winter.md). **Snowday's ground, card, muted text, red and yellow were changed once more the same day (Task 62, D54; [Answers decided on 2026-11-24, Task 62](#answers-decided-on-2026-11-24-task-62)):** Ash `#555862` and its "thirty per cent", Holly `#b33b32` and Resin `#785200` below are Task 59's, history too. Dusk's text, the swatch roles, the ink, the accent and Spruce stand.

- **The scheme, as given** (the designer did not see the image: the labels and the pixel readings came in the brief). Where label and pixel differ by one unit the label is used.

  | # | The caption's name | Printed label | Pixels | Hue, HSL saturation | Where it is used |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | 1 | soft icy grey | `#b3b6c2` | `#b3b7c2` | 228, 11 % | Dusk's muted text, exactly (Icy grey); the far end of Snowday's muted text |
  | 2 | frosted blue | `#9eb1c0` | `#9fb0c0` | 206, 21 % | Snowday's ground and card, tinted with white (55 and 90 per cent in Task 59; lighter and bluer in Task 61; about 44 and 74 per cent, the ground with extra chroma, since Task 62) |
  | 3 | muted steel blue | `#6e91af` | `#6e91af` | 208, 29 % | Dusk's accent, lifted by 38, 38 and 39 (Steel) |
  | 4 | deep slate blue | `#365675` | `#375675` | 210, 37 % | Snowday's accent, exactly (Deep slate); the far end of Dusk's card |
  | 5 | dark charcoal | `#1d2d3f` | **`#2d3039`** | 225, 12 % | the pixels' colour, as the brief says: Dusk's ground and Snowday's ink, exactly (Charcoal); the near end of Snowday's muted text. The printed `#1d2d3f` is not used |

- **What the scheme is, and the rule that follows.** Two greys (1 and 5: hue 225 to 228, 11 to 12 per cent saturated) and three blues (2, 3 and 4: hue 206 to 210, 21 to 37 per cent), all low in saturation. So **the greys speak and the blues build and mark**: text, muted text and Dusk's ground are grey; the card, Snowday's ground and the accent are blue. This is what stops a soft scheme from reading as a flat grey-blue: one blue, the accent, is the only ink with chroma (63 to 66 steps against 12 to 15 for the text colours), and it is the thing that tells you what is chosen. Charcoal is Dusk's ground and Snowday's ink, so the two themes are one set.
- **The dark ground:**
  - (a) **chosen:** swatch 5 itself, `#2d3039`. CIE lightness about 20 (not a stand-in for black), and the owner's own colour as the window's most-seen pixels;
  - (b) darker than the swatch (the brief allowed "or darker", `#22252c` and the like): more headroom for the accent and the muted text on the card, but then no pixel of the window is the owner's swatch 5, and the ground drifts toward the "tinted near-black" the guidance names;
  - (c) swatch 5 as the card over a darker ground: the same objection, and a card 1.1:1 from its ground.
  - **The cost of (a):** a lighter ground gives the other tones less room, so the dark theme's tightest margin is 13.4 per cent (row 6), where Meltwater's was 27; the card is one step (1.23:1) and the see-through bounds are weaker ([Window opacity and corners](#window-opacity-and-corners), section 3); and the ground sits next to Nord's (`#2e3440`), the nearest shipped Omarchy theme (stated in [OpenDeepCool's own theme](#opendeepcools-own-theme)).
- **The dark card:**
  - (a) swatch 4 exactly, `#365675`: 1.72:1 over the ground and the owner's own deep slate, but the accent is 3.6:1 on it and the muted text 3.8, under the 4.5 they need;
  - (b) half way from swatch 5 to swatch 4, `#324357`: 1.30:1, with the accent at 4.8 (7 per cent over its need) and the muted text at 5.0 on it;
  - (c) **chosen:** two fifths of the way, `#313f51`: 1.23:1, the accent 5.1 and the muted text 5.3. The card is bluer than the ground (hue 214, saturation 25 per cent, against 225 and 12), so it is told from the ground by hue as well as by the one step of tone. If the step reads as too faint in a real window, (b) is a one-value change.
- **The dark text:** the muted text is swatch 1 itself, so the text must go lighter than any swatch to stand a step above it. **Snowlight** `#e5ebf2` is a snow-white at the blue-greys' hue (212 degrees), 92 per cent lightness, 1.7:1 above Icy grey, and it is the colour of the lit dots. Pure white and a warm cream (the owner's complaint of Coolant) were not considered.
- **The dark accent:**
  - (a) swatch 2 as it is, `#9eb1c0`: 4.8 on the card, but it has nearly the muted text's lightness (relative luminance 0.43 and 0.47) and half the chroma of Steel (34 steps against 66), so a mark would hardly differ from a hint;
  - (b) swatch 3 as it is, `#6e91af`: 3.2 on the card, under 4.5;
  - (c) **chosen:** swatch 3 lifted by 38, 38 and 39 on its channels, `#94b7d6`: the same hue and the same chroma (66 steps against 65), at a lightness that gives 5.1 on the card and 6.3 on the ground. It is as light as the muted text and told from it by chroma, as swatch 3 is from swatch 1;
  - (d) a bluer or more saturated blue at that lightness (the stock sky blues): against the owner's "keep the character, do not invent a saturated accent".
- **Snowday's ground and card (superseded on 2026-11-24 by Task 61, and again the same day by Task 62, history):** swatch 2 tinted with white, **Hoarfrost** `#d3dce3` (55 per cent white) and **Fresh snow** `#f5f7f9` (90 per cent), 1.29:1 apart. A tint of swatch 1 would take its 228 degrees and read lilac-grey where the scheme's frost is blue; a paler ground (70 per cent white, `#e2e8ec`) gives the card about 1.15:1. Fresh snow carries a breath of the swatch's blue, so the card is not a plain white.
- **Snowday's ink:**
  - (a) **chosen:** swatch 5 itself, Charcoal `#2d3039`: 9.5:1 on the ground and 12.3 on the card (Task 59's figures; 8.6 and 11.0 since Task 62); neutral, so the accent stays the only blue ink; and the same colour as Dusk's ground;
  - (b) the printed `#1d2d3f`, a navy (10.1 and 13.0 on Task 59's ground and card; 9.2 and 11.7 on Task 62's): it has the saturation of swatch 4 (37 per cent), so ink and accent would be two navies 1.8:1 apart, and a link would differ from text by lightness alone. It is the one value to change if the owner wants a navy ink.
- **Snowday's muted text and accent:**
  - the accent is swatch 4 exactly, **Deep slate** `#365675`: 5.5:1 on the ground and 7.1 on the card (Task 59's; 5.0 and 6.4 since Task 62). A bluer, more saturated blue at the same lightness (`#2b5c88`, about 5.1) would leave the scheme's saturation (37 per cent for 52);
  - the muted text is **Ash** `#555862`, swatch 5 thirty per cent of the way to swatch 1: grey like the text, 5.1 on the ground and 6.6 on the card (Task 59's; Task 62 takes twenty-five per cent, `#4f525b`, 5.1 and 6.5 on its darker ground and card). Swatch 4 as the muted text would make a hint the accent's own colour.
  - **The cost:** the three inks sit within 1.9:1 of one another in lightness (Charcoal 0.030, Deep slate 0.087, Ash 0.098; Ash 0.085 since Task 62, so within 1.7:1). The accent is told from the text and the hint by chroma, not by lightness, as the light theme's accent was before (2.1:1 from the text in Meltwater): flagged **unverified** below.
- **The status colours** are the only other hues, and each theme keeps its hues: red 5 and 4 degrees, amber 41 and 41, green 145 and 153. They are named from the forest: **Rowan** (the berries), **Larch** (the needles in autumn) and **Lichen**, and **Holly**, **Resin** and **Spruce**. Dusk's three are lighter and Snowday's are deeper, as they must be to read on their ground (the light amber reaches 4.5:1 only when it is a dark gold; Task 62 lowered the channels of Holly and Resin by about 6 per cent, for the darker ground). The green is 63 and 57 degrees from the accent, the red about 155, and the dark theme's green and accent are equally light: a status is never colour alone, as before.
- **The control alphas: two numbers per theme changed**, which is the one place the "only colours change" rule of Task 54 is given up. The scheme's grounds are mid-toned, so the same alpha draws a weaker line: at Meltwater's alphas the dark theme's control border on a card was 3.1:1 and row 12 of the light theme 3.0 (on the edge), and the dark theme's row 12 3.4. The normal border rose from 0.44 to 0.52 (dark) and from 0.56 to 0.62 (light), and the hover border from 0.60 to 0.68 and from 0.74 to 0.78, so hover still firms a border up. Row 12 is now 4.2 and 3.5, and a border on a card 3.7 and 3.9 (the light figures are Task 59's: 3.6 and 4.0 in Task 61, 3.4 and 3.7 in Task 62). The fills, the selected fill and the pressed fill are unchanged.
- **Not changed and why:** the token set and its names; `--ground`, `--plate` and every computed rule; the selection alpha 0.25 (row 15 is 6.6 and 6.7); the radii (4, 10 and 12 px, round dots); the type; the rule that the accent only marks a choice; the sample Omarchy theme of the mockups; the window's opacity numbers. A gradient, a halo, a lit edge and a shadow stay out for the reasons of [Task 44](#answers-decided-on-2026-11-22-tasks-44-45-50-and-51).
- **What the owner should look at, as a real window:** whether the slate card reads as a card on the charcoal ground; whether a link, a hint and the text can be told apart in Snowday; Rowan as red and not as coral-pink, and Resin as gold and not as brown; Dusk beside a Nord-themed Omarchy window, which it resembles; and Glassy over a bright wallpaper, where Dusk is weaker.
- **The mockups** change in one place, the two palette blocks of `mockups-src/app.css` (the property names and the sample Omarchy theme's block are unchanged; two alpha values in each block moved), and are regenerated; `app.js` holds no palette colour. `emoji-options/chosen.html`, a gallery page with its own two lines of colours (not a generated mockup), takes the new tones as well.
- **Sources.** The scheme is the owner's, as given in the brief on 2026-11-23: Folk & Fey's "Cool Blue Forest" from its Winter Forest Collection, an Instagram post (caption: "soft icy grey, frosted blue, muted steel blue, deep slate blue, and dark charcoal tones of a snow-covered winter forest"); the designer did not fetch the post or see its image, and the labels and pixel readings are as given. Nord was read on 2026-11-23 (`/usr/share/omarchy/themes/nord/colors.toml`). The guidance is Anthropic's `frontend-design` skill as read on 2026-11-19 and 2026-11-22 ([above](#answers-decided-on-2026-11-19-task-11)); it was not read again. No palette of Coolors or Pikka is used: the previous attempt's sources are history.

### Answers decided on 2026-11-24, Tasks 60 and 61

Decided under the same delegation, for the owner's seventh list (plan D14): the app's title in the header bar, and OpenDeepCool's own light theme, for the fourth time. The designer has no shell and did not run the app. It read **a screenshot of the installed app** (the Display page in Snowday as built in Task 59, 2026-11-24) together with the pixel values the owner measured on it. Every contrast figure is a hand computation worked from the sRGB formula to six digits ([The contrast table](#the-contrast-table)), and what only a rendered window can show is marked **unverified**. The screenshot isn't kept in the repository. The owner's words are in D52 and D53.

**The title (Task 60).**
- **The size:**
  - (a) **chosen:** 1.73em, the next step of the scale's 1.2 ratio above a page's title. At the owner's examples it gives 16.8 for their 14 and 14.4 for their 12, two to three points more than the page title, against the two asked for; in the app, at a 12 px font and a 17 px page title, it is 21 px;
  - (b) 1.6em, "two more" at a 12 px font: a size no other text has, which also grows less than the others with the user's font;
  - (c) two steps, 2.07em (25 px at 12): the bar would have to grow from a 14 px font up, and the title would stand out of the scale by a whole level.
- **Does the bar hold it?** Yes. The header bar is as tall as its tallest child, the 32 px icon buttons plus the bar's own padding, and the title, a `GtkLabel` in the title slot, is centred in it. The label's line is about 1.2 times its font size (the font's own line height decides: **unverified**): about 25 px at a 12 px font, 29 at 14 and 33 at 16. So up to a 14 px font the bar's height doesn't change, and at 16 it is a pixel taller, since GTK sizes it from its children; nothing is clipped, because the label is allocated its natural height. In width, "OpenDeepCool" at 1.73em and weight 600 is about 150 px at a 12 px font; the narrowest window is the not-available message's (440 px in the mockup, which is an estimate), so the title, centred, ends about 70 px before the gear's and the close icon's box, and never meets it.
- **The tests:** `the_type_scale_has_one_size_for_each_level` pins `.app-name` at 1.73em and weight 600 as the fifth level; a window test (`--features weston-tests`) measures the title's allocation inside the bar's, its vertical middle within 1 px of the bar's and its horizontal middle within 1 px of the window's, as the page titles' test does.

**The light theme (Task 61).**

**Task 62 (2026-11-24, D54) replaced this part's colours the same day.** Every Snowday colour, step and figure below that names Hoarfrost `#d9e3ec`, Fresh snow `#f9fafb`, the 1.24:1 step, the selection `#b0c0ce` or a light contrast figure (10.1, 12.6, 5.9, 7.3 and the rest) is Task 61's, history, kept for the record; [Answers decided on 2026-11-24, Task 62](#answers-decided-on-2026-11-24-task-62) has the current ones, including the table of every element. What stands from this part: the diagnosis (points 1 to 4), the class `own-light`, its seven rules, the mapping, the frame and the fallback for the ring.

- **What is wrong, in plain words,** from the screenshot, which the owner's pixel values bear out:
  1. **One wash.** The window's ground, the left panel and the header bar are one colour, `#d3dce3`, a mid-light blue-grey with little chroma, and the only light surface on the screen is the preview's card: it reads as a bright hole in a slab, and nothing else has a surface of its own.
  2. **A lattice of outlines.** Each of the 23 tiles is a well, the ground with a 4 per cent tint (`#ccd5dc`), drawn inside the 62 per cent border (`#696f77`), which is the strongest line on the page apart from the text and the pictures' lit dots. The grid of heavy outlines is seen before the pictures it holds, and the unlit dots inside each, light grey on a grey well, add a second grey texture inside the first. It is a wireframe.
  3. **The chosen tile reads as pressed.** Its fill is the text colour at 14 per cent, a darker grey (`#bcc4cb`), and its border, the navy `#365675`, sits next to outlines of nearly the same darkness (`#696f77`): what tells it from the rest is a grey smudge, and in a light theme a darker grey is how an interface draws a pressed or an unavailable control, not a chosen one. In the dark theme the same rule draws a lighter fill, which does read as lit.
  4. **The theme has no colour of its own on this page.** The accent appears only as that 1 px border. Everything else is grey-blue at one lightness, so nothing says whose window it is.
  5. **The title is the weakest line in the bar** (1em, weight 600, next to a 17 px page title): that is D52's.
- **What isn't wrong:** the family (the blues and greys are the owner's scheme and sit well together), the text and its contrast, the status dots, and the structure of the Configuration page, whose cards are surfaces over the ground and have never had an outline. The Display page's tiles are the one place the theme draws controls on the bare ground, with the control rules meant for small controls inside cards. The ground's own tone is a minor fault (a little dull), not the cause.
- **The options.**
  - (a) colours and alphas only (a lighter ground, a softer border): a tile's outline is the shared control border and row 12 holds it at 3:1; its fill is `--fg` at 4 per cent over the ground, so it can only be a little darker than the ground. A softer border would break row 12 for every control (toggles, dropdown, switches, scales), and a lighter ground does nothing for the lattice. Rejected as the whole fix; the colours still move, because a lighter ground is what lets a card step;
  - (b) **chosen:** a window class `own-light` and seven rules under it (below), plus two colours. It changes what a tile is filled with, in the own light theme only;
  - (c) the same card rule for both own themes, with no class: it changes Dusk, which the owner did not ask about and reviewed, and the brief keeps Dusk unless a change is shared. A class `own-dark` with the same seven rules (slate cards) is the way, if the owner wants it: not done;
  - (d) a tint of the accent as the chosen fill, the first idea: the accent is a dull navy, and its 14 per cent over a white card is (222, 227, 232), within 1.01:1 of the ground's (217, 227, 236), the same tone, so the chosen tile would read as a hole in the grid; at 8 per cent it is a slightly dimmer tile, the grey smudge again in small. A frame is what a quiet palette can use: a 2 px navy line is 7.3:1 on a card;
  - (e) a token for each state of a tile (`--tile-fill`, `--tile-hover-fill` and so on) instead of a class: four to six new tokens, computed in Rust for three sources so that an Omarchy theme and Dusk keep their present values; the class gives them by construction, because no rule applies to them;
  - (f) the header bar or the left panel in the accent colour: it breaks the rule that the accent only marks what is chosen, the header's hover, pressed and selected fills are tints of the text colour written for the ground, and only the light theme would have it;
  - (g) a dark display glass for the preview in the light theme (the product is a lit matrix, and a charcoal plate with snow-white dots would be the window's one strong shape): it changes the colour rule the preview and all 23 tiles share (lit `--fg`, unlit `--fg` at 0.12) and needs tokens of its own; larger than the owner asked, and offered for later;
  - (h) a deeper frost for the left panel than for the right: two grounds, so two alphas in Glassy and a token of their own; with white tiles the window already has three tones (the ground, the card, the ink), and a fourth is decoration.
- **The colours (Task 61's, history: Task 62 replaced them, [below](#answers-decided-on-2026-11-24-task-62)).** Only two changed.
  - **Hoarfrost `#d9e3ec`** (217, 227, 236; was `#d3dce3`): hue 208 degrees (swatch 2's is 206), HSL saturation 33 per cent (the swatch's 21), CIE lightness about 90. It is swatch 2 lightened, then given back some of the chroma that lightening takes away: a plain 62 per cent white mix is `#dae1e7`, 13 steps between its lowest and highest channel against this one's 19, and reads as grey. It is as light as the ground can be and still let a card step from it: Fresh snow is 1.24:1 over it (the old pair was 1.29:1), and the same hue at `#e0e8ef` would give the card 1.18:1.
  - **Fresh snow `#f9fafb`** (249, 250, 251; was `#f5f7f9`): swatch 2 with 93 per cent white (it was 90), CIE lightness about 98. A touch whiter, so the cards, the tiles and the preview's frame read as paper over the frost, not as grey over grey.
  - **The other six colours stay:** Charcoal, Ash, Deep slate, Holly, Resin and Spruce. All pass with more room on the lighter ground: rows 1 to 10 gain 0.2 to 0.4 (10.1, 12.6, 5.5, 6.8, 5.9, 7.3, 5.4, 6.7, 4.5, 4.1) and the tightest margin of the light theme is 19 per cent (row 7), where Snowday as first built had 11.8. The owner's wish that the accent be the scheme's own swatch 4 stands.
  - **The six alphas stay,** `[0.04, 0.62, 0.07, 0.78, 0.14, 0.20]`: row 12 is 3.6 (was 3.5). The outlines of the toggles, the dropdown and the switches' troughs stay as they were; only the tile's went.
  - **The selection** becomes `#b0c0ce` (the accent at 0.25 over the new ground, 176.25, 191.75 and 206.25 rounded).
- **The mapping.** The class is set on the window with the theme, in the list that already holds `glassy` and `own-corners` (`follow.rs`, `set_theme`): `("own-light", tokens.corners.is_some() && !tokens.dark)`, which is true only for OpenDeepCool's own light theme. No token is new. The rules go in `app.css` after the control rules, in this order (the order matters where two have the same specificity: `.selected` after hover and focus, `:disabled` last, as the base rules have it):

  | Element | OpenDeepCool's own light theme (class `own-light`) | An Omarchy theme, and the dark own theme (no class) |
  |---|---|---|
  | Tile, at rest | fill `--plate`, border `--none` | fill `--ctl-normal-fill`, border `--ctl-normal-border` |
  | Tile, hover | fill `--plate`, border `--ctl-hover-border` | `--ctl-hover-fill`, `--ctl-hover-border` |
  | Tile, keyboard focus | as hover, and the 2 px `--accent` outline every control has | `--ctl-focus-fill`, `--ctl-focus-border`, and the outline |
  | Tile, pressed | fill `--plate` | `--ctl-pressed-fill` |
  | Tile, chosen (the saved mode's, with "Reading lost" too) | fill `--plate`, border `--accent`, and an inset ring of 1 px in `--accent` | `--ctl-selected-fill`, border `--accent` |
  | Tile, unavailable | fill `--ctl-normal-fill`, border `--none`, no ring; caption, flag and dots `--disabled` (Task 63; `--muted` until then) | `--ctl-normal-fill`, border `--line`, caption, flag and dots `--disabled` (Task 63; the normal border and `--muted` until then) |
  | A toggle in a card, chosen (Rotation, unit, Appearance, Window Opacity, Window Corners) | fill `--ctl-selected-fill`, border `--accent`, and the same inset ring, **not while insensitive** | `--ctl-selected-fill`, border `--accent` |
  | A chosen toggle that is insensitive (the Default Theme group while the system theme is followed) | **Task 63, D55:** `--ctl-normal-fill`, a 1 px border in `--disabled`, text `--disabled`, no ring (it was `--ctl-selected-fill`, border `--muted` and text `--fg`) | the same look: `--ctl-normal-fill`, border `--disabled`, text `--disabled` |
  | Window, header bar, left column, cards, preview's frame, popovers, banners, switches, scales, dropdown, icon buttons, links | unchanged | unchanged |

  ```css
  /* Snowday only: the tiles are cards, and what is chosen is framed (Answers decided on 2026-11-24). */
  window.own-light flowboxchild.tile {
    background-color: var(--plate);
    border-color: var(--none);
  }

  window.own-light flowboxchild.tile:hover,
  window.own-light flowboxchild.tile:focus-visible {
    background-color: var(--plate);
    border-color: var(--ctl-hover-border);
  }

  window.own-light flowboxchild.tile:active {
    background-color: var(--plate);
  }

  window.own-light flowboxchild.tile.selected {
    background-color: var(--plate);
    border-color: var(--accent);
    box-shadow: inset 0 0 0 1px var(--accent);
  }

  window.own-light flowboxchild.tile:disabled {
    background-color: var(--ctl-normal-fill);
    border-color: var(--none);
    box-shadow: none;
  }

  window.own-light .card button:checked {
    box-shadow: inset 0 0 0 1px var(--accent);
  }

  window.own-light .card button:checked:disabled {
    box-shadow: none;
  }
  ```

  - **The ring is a shadow, so nothing moves:** the tile's border stays 1 px in every state, and the inset ring is drawn inside it, so a chosen tile is a 2 px frame and its size is that of the others. It follows the border radius, so Sharp corners give a square frame.
  - **Rust:** `theme.rs`'s `LIGHT` takes the two colours ([palette-winter.md](palette-winter.md)); `follow.rs` adds the class; nothing else. The generated `:root` block is as before.
  - **An Omarchy theme keeps what it has,** to the pixel, because the class is absent and each rule starts with `window.own-light`. The one thing that does reach an Omarchy light theme is the fallback: a key its `colors.toml` lacks takes the light default's colour (Task 62's Hoarfrost or Powder snow now; Task 61's were Hoarfrost `#d9e3ec` and Fresh snow), as every palette change since Task 44 has done, and a `shell.toml` it lacks takes the six alphas, which didn't change.
- **What it costs.**
  - **Code:** one class and seven rules, no new token. A tile now has two looks (a card in Snowday, a well in Dusk and in an Omarchy theme), which the design states and the test pins.
  - **A tile has no outline at rest in Snowday:** its edge is the tone step, 1.24:1 over the ground (Task 62: 1.27:1), as the Configuration page's cards have always been. WCAG 1.4.11 asks for no boundary here: Understanding 1.4.11 (read 2026-11-24) says the criterion "does not require that controls have a visual boundary indicating the hit area", and that a boundary is "only required when there is no other visual way to identify the presence of the control". A tile is a picture with its caption. What the criterion does ask, the states, keeps its 3:1: the chosen frame is 7.3:1 on a card and 5.9:1 on the ground (rows 6 and 5; Task 62: 6.4 and 5.0), the focus ring is the same accent, and the hover border is the control's own.
  - **The two own themes draw a tile differently:** Dusk's are still wells with outlines. If the owner wants Dusk's tiles to be cards, it's `own-dark` and the same rules (option c).
  - **The window modes.** *Opaque* (the default): as drawn. *Transparent* (0.985): no visible change. *Glassy* (0.75): a tile is `--plate`, as dense as a card (0.94 over the ground, [Window opacity and corners](#window-opacity-and-corners)); the hover border, the frame and the ring are opaque; an unavailable tile's `--ctl-normal-fill` is the same translucent tint as before. *Sharp corners:* tiles, rings and toggles are square. With an Omarchy theme the window mode is Omarchy's and no rule here applies.
- **Every element in Task 61's Snowday, checked** (**history: the colours and figures of this table are Task 61's; Task 62's table is [below](#answers-decided-on-2026-11-24-task-62)**; hand figures, 2026-11-24, from the rows of [the contrast table](#the-contrast-table) and the pairs outside it; the owner's list of elements, in the order the window shows them):

  | Element | How it reads now | The pair, and its figure |
  |---|---|---|
  | Window, left column, header bar | Hoarfrost, one ground; a 1 px rule (`--fg` at 0.12) under the header and beside the column | text on it 10.1 (row 1) |
  | App title, page titles, category headings | Charcoal, weight 600, at 1.73em, 1.44em and 1.2em | as text, 10.1 on the ground and 12.6 on a card |
  | Preview's frame | Fresh snow, opaque in every mode, the picture's plate; the only card that is not `--plate` | lit against unlit dot on it 10.1 (outside the table) |
  | Tile, at rest | a card (`--plate`), no outline; the picture's unlit dots are faint on it | caption 12.6 (row 2); the edge, Fresh snow on Hoarfrost, 1.24 |
  | Tile, hover | the card with the control's hover border | border over the card 6.4 |
  | Tile, chosen | the card with the 2 px `--accent` frame | frame 7.3 on a card, 5.9 on the ground (rows 6, 5) |
  | Tile, keyboard focus | as hover, and the 2 px `--accent` outline at 2 px offset | 5.9 on the ground (row 5) |
  | Tile, unavailable | the recessed well (`--fg` at 0.04 over the ground), no outline; caption and dashes in `--muted` | muted on the well about 5.1, `--fg` on it 9.5 (not required of a disabled control) |
  | Status dots and their words | Spruce, Resin, Holly, or the hollow `--muted` ring, each with its words | green 4.1, red 4.5 on the ground (rows 10, 9); the words are text, 10.1 |
  | Configuration cards (Theme, Display, Music, Startup) | Fresh snow over Hoarfrost, no outline, as ever | card edge 1.24; text 12.6 (row 2), hints 6.8 (row 4) |
  | Chosen toggle in a card (Rotation, unit, Appearance, Window Opacity, Window Corners) | `--fg` at 0.14 over the card, the 1 px `--accent` border and the inset ring: a 2 px frame | text on the fill 9.8 (row 11); frame 7.3 |
  | Idle toggle, dropdown button, switch off, scale trough | the card with `--fg` at 0.04 and the 62 per cent border | border over the ground 3.6 (row 12), over a card 4.0 |
  | Switch on, scale fill | the Deep slate trough or fill, a charcoal knob with a 1 px Hoarfrost ring | trough against the ground 5.9, against a card 7.3 (rows 5, 6); the ring on the trough 5.9; the knob alone 1.7, which the ring answers |
  | Open GPU list, notice | Fresh snow, opaque, with the 1 px rule | text 12.6 |
  | Banners | the status colour at 0.16 or 0.14 over the ground, the icon in the status colour, the text in `--fg` | text on the tints 8.1 and 8.4; icons 3.6 and 4.4 (against 3.0). The tint is a dull grey-green for a warning, unchanged here |
  | The Default Theme card | a child of the Theme card; dimmed: heading, titles and buttons `--muted`, the saved value's button still readable, with no ring (**history: Task 63, D55, replaced this look with the disabled look**) | muted on a card 6.8 (row 4) |
  | Frameless buttons (gear, close, back, Refresh), links | no frame; hover `--fg` at 0.07, pressed 0.20, the gear's chosen fill 0.14 with an `--accent` icon; links `--accent`, underlined | icon on the chosen fill 4.6 (row 14); link 5.9 on the ground and 7.3 on a card |
  | Hint and note text | `--muted`, 0.83em | 5.5 on the ground (row 3), 6.8 on a card (row 4) |
  | Selected text | `--accent` at 0.25 over the ground, `#b0c0ce` | text on it 7.1 (row 15) |

- **What the owner should look at, in a real window** (Task 61's list; the owner's verdict on it was "too bright", and Task 62's list is [below](#answers-decided-on-2026-11-24-task-62); the designer didn't run the app): (1) whether the white tiles read as cards on the 1.24:1 ground; if the step is too faint, the ground goes to `#d5dfe9` (1.29:1, the old step), at the price of rows 3, 7 and 10 falling to about 5.3, 5.2 and 4.0, all still passing; (2) whether the 2 px navy frame is enough, or too much, for the chosen tile; (3) whether the hover outline (the 78 per cent border) is too heavy for a pointer that only passes over; `--ctl-normal-border` is the softer choice and is one word; (4) whether the ground reads as a frosted blue and not as grey; (5) the warning banner, whose tint is the status colour at 14 per cent over the ground, a dull grey-green strip with Resin's brown icon: the tint rule is shared by every theme and is not changed here.
- **The mockups** change in `mockups-src/app.css` only: the two colours of the light block, the `--fs-title` variable and `.app-name`, and the seven Snowday rules (the same selectors with `:root[data-theme="light"]` in front of the tile and toggle, using `--plate`, which the mockup's window already computes). `app.js` holds no colour. The generated pages are regenerated by `odc-mockups`.
- **The tests (Task 61):** `PALETTES` takes the two colours; the 15 figures and the muted-on-selected note are re-pinned ([palette-winter.md](palette-winter.md)); a window test with weston puts the class on the window and samples a tile at rest (the card's fill, no outline pixel), on hover (the border), chosen (the frame's two pixels, the outermost and the next one in, both `--accent`) and unavailable (the well); and the same samples under an Omarchy theme give the old pixels. If the pinned GTK doesn't draw the inset ring (unverified, below), the test says so and the fallback below applies.
- **The fallback for the ring:** if `box-shadow: inset` doesn't draw on a `GtkFlowBoxChild` or a `GtkToggleButton` in the pinned GTK, the implementer reports it (rule 2) and the frame is made by `border-width: 2px` on the tiles and the toggles under `own-light` (transparent at rest), which makes a tile 2 px larger each way, 12 px more across a row of six.
- **Sources.** The screenshot and its pixel values are the owner's, read 2026-11-24; the designer didn't take it. WCAG 2.2, Understanding 1.4.11 (w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/non-text-contrast.html, read 2026-11-24). GTK's CSS reference (docs.gtk.org/gtk4/css-properties.html, read 2026-11-24) lists `box-shadow` as CSS Backgrounds Level 3 without syntax; `inset` is that specification's.

### Answers decided on 2026-11-24, Task 62

Decided under the same delegation, for the owner's eighth list (plan D15): "the light theme is too bright now", said on opening Task 61's build. The designer has no shell and did not run the app. It read **two pictures of the real window**, a screenshot of the installed app (the Configuration page, with the new title) and the Display page's tiles as the app's own window test renders them with the same stylesheet, and measured them. Every figure is a hand computation worked from the sRGB formula to six digits ([The contrast table](#the-contrast-table)), and what only a rendered window can show is marked **unverified**. Neither picture is kept in the repository. The owner's words are in D54.

**What is too bright, in plain words, measured** (Task 61's colours; lightness is CIE L\*, from the relative luminance Y):

| Surface | Colour | Y | L\* | Where it is |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Card, tile, preview's frame (Fresh snow) | `#f9fafb` | 0.955 | 98.2 | the four Configuration cards, the 23 tiles, the frame over the status items |
| Ground (Hoarfrost) | `#d9e3ec` | 0.757 | 89.7 | the left column, the header, the page margins |
| An unlit dot on the frame | `#e1e2e4` | 0.758 | 89.7 | the 196 dots of the preview, and of every tile |
| Ink (Charcoal) | `#2d3039` | 0.030 | 19.9 | text, the lit dots |
| Hint (Ash) | `#555862` | 0.098 | 37.5 | hints, notes |

1. **The card is white.** At 0.955 it is 95 per cent of the display's white, and the blue it carries is 2 steps of 255 between its channels, too little for an eye to read as a colour. Every card, tile and the preview's frame is paper white in a window whose ground is itself pale.
2. **It fills the window.** In the screenshot (1041 × 957 px) the four Configuration cards cover about 560 000 px² and the frame 51 000: **61 per cent of the window is L\* 98**, and the area-weighted lightness is L\* 95.1 (luminance 0.878). The Display page has its 23 tiles for the cards and the same frame (the share is not measured, and is over a third of the window).
3. **The ground gives no relief.** It is only 8.5 points of L\* under the card, and the picture's unlit dots are as light as the ground (89.7 on 98.2), so the left column is one pale field with a white card in it.
4. **The ink adds the glare.** Charcoal on the card is 12.6:1, the strongest edge in the window: a near-white field against near-black ink is the pair a bright room makes hard on the eyes. The hint (6.8) and the accent (7.3) are not the cause.

**What isn't too bright:** the structure. The class `own-light`, the tiles as cards, the 2 px frame for what is chosen, the card step of the Configuration page: the screenshot shows them doing what D53 asked. The cause is the tones.

**The aim, and its floor.** A calm mid-light theme: the scheme's frost at about L\* 84 for the ground and 93 for the cards, so that the window's mean lightness falls by about 5 points, with the step between card and ground as large as before. The owner's own swatches have nothing lighter than L\* 73 (swatch 1), so near-white was never the scheme's. **The floor is the accent.** Swatch 4, `#365675` (Y 0.087), is the accent and the owner's colour, and it needs 4.5:1 against the ground: the ground can't be darker than Y 0.568 (L\* 80), and Y 0.61 (L\* 82) leaves a 6 per cent margin. The ground goes to Y 0.638, which gives it 5.0.

**The options.**
- (a) the card only: at L\* 92.8 over the old ground (Y 0.757) the step is 1.08:1 and the tiles stop being cards; to keep 1.2:1 the card must stay at L\* 96.5, which is not calmer. Rejected;
- (b) the ground only: the cards, 60 per cent of the window, stay white, and the step grows to 1.46:1 with a dark ground around a white sheet. Rejected;
- (c) **chosen:** both, the ground to L\* 83.8 and the card to L\* 92.8: the step is 1.27:1 (ΔL\* 9.0; it was 1.24:1 and 8.5), a little firmer than Task 61's, so the tiles keep reading as cards, and the mean lightness is L\* 89.5 (luminance 0.752), 5.6 points and 14 per cent below Task 61's;
- (d) a more saturated card (for example `#dfe9f3`, hue 210, saturation 45 per cent): a blue plate on a blue ground is Task 59's flat wash again, and what makes a card is tone, not hue; the saturation also competes with the accent, the one ink with chroma. Rejected;
- (e) a ground at L\* 80 or below, nearer swatch 2's own L\* 71: the accent would be at its 4.5 need with no margin, and the muted text and the warning text would have to go darker or browner, and the accent is the owner's swatch. Rejected;
- (f) a smaller move (ground L\* 86 and card L\* 94, which keeps Ash and Resin as they are): the mean lightness would fall by about 4 points. After five rounds on this theme a move that may be read as "still too bright" costs more than one that goes a clear step, so it is not chosen; it is the dial the other way (the first item of what the owner should look at, below).

- **The colours.** Five of the eight change; the accent and the ink are the owner's swatches and stay.
  - **Hoarfrost `#c4d3e1`** (196, 211, 225; was `#d9e3ec`): hue 209, HSL saturation 33 per cent (as before), Y 0.638, L\* 83.8. Swatch 2 (158, 177, 192) lightened by about 44 per cent white, then given back the chroma that lightening takes away, as in Task 61: 29 steps between its lowest and highest channel, where a plain mix at that lightness (`#c9d3dc`) has 19 and reads as grey;
  - **Powder snow `#e6ebf1`** (230, 235, 241; was Fresh snow `#f9fafb`): hue 213, saturation 28 per cent, Y 0.826, L\* 92.8. Swatch 2 with about 74 per cent white and a touch more blue than the plain mix (`#e6ebef`): it is the card, the tile and the preview's frame. It is named for what it is: a pale cool tint, no longer a fresh white. The step over the ground is 1.27:1;
  - **Ash `#4f525b`** (79, 82, 91; was `#555862`): swatch 5 twenty-five per cent of the way to swatch 1 (it was thirty). On the darker ground the old Ash is 4.6:1, 3 per cent over its need; this is 5.1. It is still a neutral grey (saturation 7 per cent), so the accent stays the only blue ink, and 1.7:1 from the text in lightness (it was 1.9);
  - **Holly `#a9322a`** (169, 50, 42; was `#b33b32`): the same hue, its channels about 6 per cent lower. The error banner's icon is Holly on Holly at 16 per cent over the ground, and the ground is darker now: the old Holly's icon would be 3.1:1 against its 3.0, this one's is 3.5;
  - **Resin `#704d00`** (112, 77, 0; was `#785200`): the same hue, its channels about 6 per cent lower. The warning text on the ground would be 4.6 with the old Resin; it is 5.0. It is a dark gold, and what the owner should look at is whether it reads as gold and not as brown (it was borderline before, and a dark amber that reaches 5:1 on a mid-light ground has to be this dark);
  - **Charcoal, Deep slate and Spruce stay.** Spruce is 3.5 on the ground (4.1 before), the thinnest margin a status dot has, 17.7 per cent over its 3.0;
  - **The six alphas stay,** `[0.04, 0.62, 0.07, 0.78, 0.14, 0.20]`: row 12 is 3.4 (was 3.6), 12.6 per cent over its need, and a control's border on a card 3.7. The selection alpha 0.25 stays; the selection becomes `#a1b4c6` (160.5, 179.75 and 198 rounded half away from zero: the red channel is a tie, as `blend` rounds it).

**Does any rule change?** No. The class `own-light`, its seven rules, the 2 px frame, the mapping in `follow.rs`, the tokens, the radii and the type are Task 61's and Task 60's. Nothing in `app.css` of the app changes, only the colours the rules use. The dark theme and every Omarchy theme are untouched (the only reach is the fallback of a key an Omarchy light theme's files lack, which takes the light default's colour as before).

**The 15 rows.** All pass ([the contrast table](#the-contrast-table); [palette-winter.md](palette-winter.md) has the figures to three decimals and the ones near a rounding edge). The light column was `[10.1, 12.6, 5.5, 6.8, 5.9, 7.3, 5.4, 6.7, 4.5, 4.1, 9.8, 3.6, 8.2, 4.6, 7.1]` and is `[8.6, 11.0, 5.1, 6.5, 5.0, 6.4, 5.0, 6.4, 4.3, 3.5, 8.6, 3.4, 7.1, 4.0, 6.2]`. The tightest margins are row 7 (the warning text on the ground, 5.0 against 4.5, 11.1 per cent), row 5 (the accent on the ground, 5.0, 11.3), row 12 (the control border, 3.4 against 3.0, 12.6) and row 3 (the hint on the ground, 5.1, 13.6). The light theme is the tighter of the two now (the dark theme's tightest is 13.4 per cent): a mid-light ground costs margin, and it is the price of the change.

**Every element in Snowday now, checked** (hand figures, 2026-11-24, from the rows of [the contrast table](#the-contrast-table) and the pairs outside it; the owner's list of elements, in the order the window shows them):

| Element | How it reads now | The pair, and its figure |
|---|---|---|
| Window, left column, header bar | Hoarfrost, one ground; a 1 px rule (`--fg` at 0.12) under the header and beside the column | text on it 8.6 (row 1) |
| App title, page titles, category headings | Charcoal, weight 600, at 1.73em, 1.44em and 1.2em | as text, 8.6 on the ground and 11.0 on a card |
| Preview's frame | Powder snow, opaque in every mode, the picture's plate; the only card that is not `--plate`; the unlit dots `#d0d5db` on it | lit against unlit dot on it 8.9 (outside the table); unlit dot against the frame 1.24 |
| Tile, at rest | a card (`--plate`), no outline; the picture's unlit dots are faint on it | caption 11.0 (row 2); the edge, Powder snow on Hoarfrost, 1.27 |
| Tile, hover | the card with the control's hover border | border over the card 5.8 |
| Tile, chosen | the card with the 2 px `--accent` frame | frame 6.4 on a card, 5.0 on the ground (rows 6, 5) |
| Tile, keyboard focus | as hover, and the 2 px `--accent` outline at 2 px offset | 5.0 on the ground (row 5) |
| Tile, unavailable | the recessed well (`--fg` at 0.04 over the ground), no outline; caption, flag and dashes in `--disabled` (Task 63; `--muted` until then) | `--disabled` on the well 2.3 (was `--muted` 4.8; not required of a disabled control), `--fg` on it 8.1 |
| Status dots and their words | Spruce, Resin, Holly, or the hollow `--muted` ring, each with its words | green 3.5, red 4.3 on the ground (rows 10, 9); the words are text, 8.6 |
| The warning text (the reason line under the preview, the notes) | `--yellow` | 5.0 on the ground (row 7), 6.4 on a card (row 8) |
| Configuration cards (Theme, Display, Music, Startup) | Powder snow over Hoarfrost, no outline, as ever | card edge 1.27; text 11.0 (row 2), hints 6.5 (row 4) |
| Chosen toggle in a card (Rotation, unit, Appearance, Window Opacity, Window Corners) | `--fg` at 0.14 over the card, the 1 px `--accent` border and the inset ring: a 2 px frame | text on the fill 8.6 (row 11); frame 6.4 |
| Idle toggle, dropdown button, switch off, scale trough | the card with `--fg` at 0.04 and the 62 per cent border | border over the ground 3.4 (row 12), over a card 3.7 |
| Switch on, scale fill | the Deep slate trough or fill, a charcoal knob with a 1 px Hoarfrost ring | trough against the ground 5.0, against a card 6.4 (rows 5, 6); the ring on the trough 5.0; the knob alone 1.7, which the ring answers |
| Open GPU list, notice | Powder snow, opaque, with the 1 px rule | text 11.0 |
| Banners | the status colour at 0.16 or 0.14 over the ground (a grey-mauve and a grey-teal strip, `#c0b9c4` and `#b8c0c2`), the icon in the status colour, the text in `--fg` | text on the tints 6.9 and 7.1; icons 3.5 and 4.1 (against 3.0). The tint rule is shared by every theme and is not changed here |
| The Default Theme card | a child of the Theme card; **disabled while the system theme is followed (Task 63, D55):** heading, titles and button text `--disabled`, hairline borders, no accent, and a 1 px `--disabled` frame round the saved value; live otherwise | `--disabled` on a card 3.1 (was `--muted`, row 4: 6.5); the frame 3.1 ([the Task 63 table](#answers-decided-on-2026-11-24-task-63)) |
| Frameless buttons (gear, close, back, Refresh), links | no frame; hover `--fg` at 0.07, pressed 0.20, the gear's chosen fill 0.14 with an `--accent` icon; links `--accent`, underlined | icon on the chosen fill 4.0 (row 14); link 5.0 on the ground and 6.4 on a card |
| Hint and note text | `--muted`, 0.83em | 5.1 on the ground (row 3), 6.5 on a card (row 4) |
| Selected text | `--accent` at 0.25 over the ground, `#a1b4c6` | text on it 6.2 (row 15) |

**The window modes.** *Opaque* (the default): as drawn. *Transparent* (0.985): no visible change. *Glassy* (0.75): the ground is Hoarfrost at 0.75 over the backdrop and a card or tile is Powder snow at 0.94; over the worst backdrop for a light theme, uniform black, the bounds are the light column of [the Glassy table](#window-opacity-and-corners) (text on the bare ground 4.8, on a card 9.2, the hint on a card 5.4, the accent on a card 5.3, the hint or the accent on the bare ground 2.9 and 2.8). The two lower ones can't be promised, as with every theme: the bare ground carries the captions and the reason lines, each of which has its words. A darker ground has a little less room under a black backdrop than a pale one: Task 61's bare-ground text was 5.6, and its hint on the bare ground 3.0. *Sharp corners:* as before. With an Omarchy theme the window mode is Omarchy's, and no colour here applies.

**What it costs.**
- **Thinner margins:** the light theme's tightest is 11.1 per cent, where Task 61's was 19 and the dark theme's is 13.4. Six rows are within 0.02 of a rounding edge (1, 8, 10, 13, 14 and 15) and may flip by a tenth in the test, a pin to change and not a fault.
- **A smaller gap between the ink and the hint** in lightness, 1.7:1 where it was 1.9. The hint is also smaller and stands under its title, so it is still told from the text, and from the accent by chroma (63 steps against 12) as before; this stays **unverified** in a rendered window.
- **Two status colours are deeper,** Holly and Resin. Resin is a dark gold that may read as brown on some panels; Holly is a brick red. Both are the same hue as before.
- **The card is no longer paper:** Powder snow is a pale cool tint at L\* 92.8. The risk is Task 59's, a flat wash. What answers it is the same as in Task 61: the ground has more chroma than the card (29 steps against 11), the frame for what is chosen is the accent, the ink is the darkest thing in the window, and the pictures' lit dots are `--fg`. The dark theme has the same tone structure at 1.23:1.
- **Glassy over a black backdrop** is a little weaker for the bare ground (above).
- **Code:** the same as Task 61's, less the class: five colours in `theme.rs`, the tests' pins and one test constant.

**What the owner should look at, in a real window, and the dials** (the designer didn't run the app; the dial figures are hand computations to a tenth, not the six digits of the table, and a dial is a change of values the implementer makes and recomputes):
1. **Is it calm now, and not dull?** If it is still too bright: one step darker, ground `#bfcfde` and card `#e2e8ef` (about 1.4 and 1.1 points of L\* lower, 1.29:1 apart). Every row still passes, the tightest being rows 5 and 7 at 4.8 against 4.5. That is the floor: below it the accent, the owner's swatch 4, no longer has room. If it is dull or too dark: one step lighter, ground `#c9d7e4` and card `#e9eef3` (about 1.5 and 1.0 points lighter, 1.26:1 apart), with row 5 at 5.2.
2. **Do the tiles still read as cards** on the 1.27:1 ground? If the step is too faint, the card goes to `#eaeff4` (1.32:1, L\* 94.2); rows 2, 4, 6 and 8 rise, and nothing falls.
3. **Is the 2 px frame** the right weight on the new tones, now 6.4:1 on a card (was 7.3)?
4. **Does Resin read as gold and not as brown, and Holly as red?** If Resin reads as brown, the only way to a brighter gold is a lighter ground (the dial above).
5. **Does the ground read as a frosted blue and not as grey or as plain blue?** It has 29 steps of chroma at hue 209.
6. **The error banner:** the tint is now a grey-mauve (`#c0b9c4`), with Holly's deeper icon on it.

**The mockups** change in `mockups-src/app.css` only: the light block's five colours and its comment (`--bg`, `--surface`, `--muted`, `--red` and `--yellow`); and `emoji-options/chosen.html`, a gallery page with two lines of colours of its own, takes the new ground and muted text in its light line. No rule, no variable and nothing in `app.js` changes. The generated pages are regenerated by `odc-mockups`.

**The tests (Task 62):** `PALETTES`' "default light" takes the five colours (`background` `#c4d3e1`, `surface` `#e6ebf1`, `muted` `#4f525b`, `red` `#a9322a`, `yellow` `#704d00`; the alphas, `selected` 0.14 and `border` 0.62, don't change); the light array of `the_default_theme_has_the_contrast_the_design_states`, in the mockups' test and in `theme.rs`'s, becomes `[8.6, 11.0, 5.1, 6.5, 5.0, 6.4, 5.0, 6.4, 4.3, 3.5, 8.6, 3.4, 7.1, 4.0, 6.2]` and the light note on the muted text on the selected fill 5.1; `theme.rs`'s `LIGHT` takes the five colours and its comment names Powder snow and Task 62; the window test's `SNOWDAY_CARD` becomes `#e6ebf1` (the accent constant stays). The dark theme's figures don't change, and no `own-light` rule changes.

**Sources.** The two pictures are the owner's installed app and the app's own window test, read 2026-11-24; the designer didn't take them. Relative luminance and the contrast ratio are WCAG 2's; CIE lightness is CIE 1976 L\* from the luminance. The scheme is the owner's, Folk & Fey's "Cool Blue Forest" ([Task 59's sources](#answers-decided-on-2026-11-23-task-59)). No palette from outside was used.

### Answers decided on 2026-11-24, Task 63

Decided under the same delegation, for the owner's ninth list (plan D16): "I still dont see when the system theme is enabled, the default theme being disabled... to user understand this, all the default theme and its items, buttons, should use the disabled color. The user should be able to see and understand that that section and its contents are disabled." The designer has no shell and did not run the app. It read **two renders of the followed state**, light and dark (the Configuration page, "Use system theme" on, no header bar in the headless render, taken from the app's own window test with the same stylesheet), and the stylesheet. Every contrast figure is a hand computation worked from the sRGB formula to six digits, and what only a rendered window can show is marked **unverified**. The renders are not kept in the repository. The owner's words are in D55.

**What the renders show.** The section is its heading, three titles and seven buttons (the brief said nine: the three rows hold 2 + 3 + 2). The Use system theme row above it is live.

1. **The heading and the three titles are `--muted`**, the colour of the hints and notes: 5.3:1 (dark) and 6.5:1 (light) on the card. A person reads that as secondary text, not as off; in the light render the only hint is that the titles are a little lighter than "Use system theme" above them.
2. **Each button is the live button with muted text**: the normal fill and the normal border (`--ctl-normal-border`, 3.7:1 against the card), so Dark and Light look like the live Rotation and Temperature unit buttons just below.
3. **The pressed button is as strong as a live pressed one**: the selected fill, `--fg` text (5.8:1 on the fill in the dark theme, 8.6 in the light one) and a `--muted` border; in the dark render Dark, Opaque and Rounded look live.
4. **Nothing else says off:** the outlines, the fills and the accent are all as in a live control. The look of D44 and Task 11 ("dimmed") and D43 (muted text on the normal fill and border) are the cause, and **this task overturns them**: D44 reasoned that "dimming is a muted colour", and a muted colour is exactly what a hint is.

**What a disabled look is, by the platform and by WCAG.**
- libadwaita draws a disabled widget at `--disabled-opacity`, **50 per cent** (40 per cent in high contrast), the whole widget alike, text, border and fill: its stylesheet variables page says "`--disabled-opacity` is used for disabled widgets (see `GtkWidget:sensitive`)" and lists `--dim-opacity` (55 per cent) apart (gnome.pages.gitlab.gnome.org/libadwaita/doc/main/css-variables.html, read 2026-11-24). A disabled widget there is half of everything, which is why it reads as off where a dimmed label next to live outlines doesn't. GNOME's HIG page on disabled widgets was **not read**.
- WCAG 2.2 exempts "Text or images of text that are part of an inactive user interface component" from 1.4.3, defined as "visible but not currently operable" (Understanding 1.4.3, read 2026-11-24), and an inactive component from 1.4.11 (read 2026-11-24, Task 61). So no contrast is required of the section. The design still wants it legible, and wants a person to find which value is saved, which is the design's own aim and not WCAG's.

**What the Omarchy case needs, which decides the colour's derivation.** The section is disabled whenever the system theme is followed, and with Omarchy that is the owner's everyday window. The token must be derived, because an Omarchy `colors.toml` has no key for it. And it must not be derived from `muted`, because that key is whatever the theme gives it: read from the shipped themes' `colors.toml` on 2026-11-24 (`/usr/share/omarchy/themes/*/colors.toml`), the five whose `muted` I worked by hand are at **1.3 to 1.6:1** against their `lighter_background`, and others look much higher by eye (a mid-grey on a near-black card; not worked). A disabled colour made from `muted` would be invisible in many themes and strong in others. One made from the theme's `foreground` over its `lighter_background` (the card) is always the middle of that theme's own range, whatever it is: **2.3 to 4.0:1 on the card across the six shipped themes I worked** (a dark theme with a soft text colour on its card gives 2.3, a black-on-grey light one 4.0).

**The options.**
- (a) `opacity: .5` on every disabled widget and label, as libadwaita does: the app has no `opacity` (D43), and a widget's opacity over a card that is itself see-through in Glassy lets the backdrop into the text, a second unknown beside the card's, where an opaque colour is a known pair against the card (the contrast table's own). Rejected for that, not because it wouldn't work;
- (b) keep `--muted` and add a cue: a lock icon, the word "off" in the heading, a box round the section. The colour is the fault; an icon or a word is decoration or a new string, and a lock says "protected", not "off". Rejected;
- (c) **chosen:** a computed disabled colour for text, icon and knob, a hairline for the border, a flat fill, no accent, and, for what is chosen or on, a **shape** in the disabled colour;
- (d) one fixed grey in every theme: it would clash with the card's tint, which a warm Omarchy theme and the cool Dusk don't share. Rejected;
- (e) `--muted` blended further toward the card: depends on the theme's `muted`, which is the unpredictable key above. Rejected.

**How the saved value stays findable, when everything is dimmed.**
- (i) the selected fill under `--disabled` text: the fill is a tint of the text colour, so it takes the label's contrast from 2.82 to **1.83:1** (dark) and from 3.13 to **2.44:1** (light), and the patch it makes (1.54:1 and 1.28:1 against the card) is the faintest cue of the set. Rejected: the one label the person wants to read gets the worst contrast;
- (ii) **chosen:** **a 1 px frame in `--disabled`**, with the normal fill, no accent and no ring. The label keeps 2.82 and 3.13:1, the frame is itself 2.82 and 3.13:1 (the strongest mark of the section and, in the light theme, over the 3:1 a live component's boundary would need), and the unpressed buttons have the hairline at 1.38 and 1.24:1, so the saved value is the one framed button of its row: a shape, not a strength. The Snowday 2 px frame and its ring are off while insensitive, as before;
- (iii) a check mark before the label: it needs an image child, and the button's width changes between states, so the group would jump when the section goes live or off. Rejected;
- (iv) weight 600 on the saved value: the same width change, and a heavier label is the strength the owner asked to remove. Rejected;
- (v) a dashed frame: GTK's CSS reference doesn't state which `border-style` values it draws (docs.gtk.org, CSS Properties, read 2026-11-24: **unverified**), and a dashed frame on linked, rounded buttons is busy. Rejected;
- (vi) an inverted chip (a `--disabled` fill with `--surface` text): the heaviest mark in the card, louder than the live chosen look, which contradicts "disabled recedes". Rejected.

**Which other widgets take the look, and what it costs.** The owner's request is the section; the shared rules are the smallest change that leaves the app with one disabled look, because the section's buttons are plain `button:disabled` and the rest are other `:disabled` rules in the same stylesheet. **Every insensitive control adopts it.** The three scopes weighed: (1) the section alone, which leaves a grey section beside a disabled switch with a bright accent trough and tiles in `--muted`, two looks; (2) **chosen:** every insensitive control, with a title dimmed only as part of a section; (3) (2) and also the title of every row whose single control is insensitive, which flickers a title for the seconds of a Start at boot flip and adds a class to two more labels for no gain the switch's own grey doesn't give.

| Element | When it is insensitive | The look | What it costs |
|---|---|---|---|
| The Default Theme heading, three titles and seven buttons | the system theme is followed | the table of [Theme card](#theme-card) | the section's whole point |
| The Use system theme switch | no system theme found (off) | normal fill, hairline trough, `--disabled` knob; its title stays `--fg` | the switch was the live switch with a muted knob |
| The Start at boot switch | a flip runs, the state is unknown, the display service is starting | off: as above; on: a trough filled `--disabled` and a `--surface` knob, no ring; the handle and trough still show the flip | **it was the `--accent` trough with a muted knob.** The window is grey for the seconds a flip takes and live again after; a dismissed dialog returns it at once. This is the change most likely to surprise |
| An unavailable tile, Dusk and an Omarchy theme | the metric has no value and the tile isn't the saved mode | the normal fill, the hairline border, caption, flag and lit dots `--disabled` | the tile loses its outline: a ghost. Its caption was `--muted` |
| An unavailable tile, Snowday | the same | the well (the normal fill, no outline) as built; caption, flag and dots `--disabled` | the caption falls from 4.8 to **2.3:1** on the well; the group's reason line, in `--muted`, is the readable text |
| The gear | the service isn't running | icon `--disabled`, no fill | on the ground the icon is 3.47:1 (Dusk) and 2.46:1 (Snowday) |
| A dropdown button, a scale | never today; the GPU row and the scales are on a page that isn't reachable when the service is down | the same rules: hairline border, `--disabled` text, knob and filled bar | none |
| **Not changed** | | `--muted` stays on hints, notes, reason lines and the hollow status dot; "Using the Omarchy theme." under the switch stays `--muted`; a row's title beside one insensitive switch stays `--fg` | A title dims with its section but not with its single control: the switch carries the state, the title is what a person reads to know which switch it is, and a flip's seconds would flicker it. One class on each of two labels extends it |

**The code is small.** About 35 lines of the stylesheet change or arrive (six shared rules and three for the switch and the scale), one token (a method, two constants, one line in `root()`), the section's class name (`muted` to `disabled`, in `show_theme`, and its test), the tile's dot colour (a field renamed and read from the token), and the tests below. No layout, size, motion, name, role or Tab order changes. **Risks, in order:** the Start at boot look above; `switch:disabled:checked > slider` and `.linked button:checked:disabled` matching in GTK, which the pixel test shows (their fallbacks are in [Unverified points](#unverified-points-answered-phase-11a-task-63-2026-11-24)); and a theme whose `mode` disagrees with its colours, which gets the other scheme's alpha (a change of a few tenths of a ratio).

**The token.** `--disabled` is `--fg` over `--surface`, opaque, rounded per channel like `blend`, **40 per cent in a dark theme and 55 per cent in a light one**. Two numbers, because the same share gives a light card a smaller luminance step: 40 and 55 per cent put the disabled text at CIE lightness **54.7 and 55.1**, the middle of the scale in both own themes (what a 50 per cent opacity gives on pure black or white, and so what libadwaita's half gives), and 2.8 and 3.1:1 on the card. A single 0.50 would give 3.5 and 2.7, with the dark text at L\* 61, only 12 below the muted text (73), where 40 per cent gives 18. An Omarchy theme takes its own `foreground`, `lighter_background` and `mode` (`Tokens::dark`): no key, nothing to read, nothing to log.

| Theme | `--fg` | `--surface` | Share | `--disabled` | Relative luminance | On `--surface` | On `--bg` | `--fg` against it | `--muted` on `--surface`, for comparison |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Dusk, the own dark theme | `#e5ebf2` | `#313f51` | 0.40 | `#798491` (121, 132, 145) | 0.226118 | **2.817** | 3.466 | 3.168 | 5.298 |
| Snowday, the own light theme | `#2d3039` | `#e6ebf1` | 0.55 | `#80848c` (128, 132, 140) | 0.229852 | **3.130** | 2.457 | 3.512 | 6.511 |
| The mockups' sample Omarchy theme, dark (made up) | `#e4dccb` | `#2a2c2a` | 0.40 | `#74726a` (116, 114, 106) | 0.167969 | 2.92 | 3.36 | | |
| A made-up light Omarchy theme (`#f4f0e8` ground, `#e6e0d4` card, `#34302b` text) | `#34302b` | `#e6e0d4` | 0.55 | `#847f77` (132, 127, 119) | 0.214174 | 3.02 | 3.50 | | |

Other figures worked by hand, on the card: **the hairline** `--line` (`--fg` at 0.12) is `#475464` (1.381:1, dark) and `#d0d5db` (1.236:1, light); **the live unpressed border** is 3.675 and 3.743; **a tile's text** on the unavailable tile of Dusk 3.033 and on Snowday's well 2.294; and the label on a selected fill, had it been used, 1.833 and 2.440. **Under Omarchy's Window Opacity** (the section is disabled while an Omarchy theme is followed, so this is the case that matters; the own themes are opaque and rounded while followed, so their Glassy figures concern only the other disabled parts), the text on the card:

| | Opaque | Transparent (0.985) | Glassy (0.75), the worst backdrop |
|---|---|---|---|
| The sample Omarchy theme, dark | 2.92 | 2.9 | 2.49 (white behind) |
| The made-up light Omarchy theme | 3.02 | 3.0 | 2.72 (black behind) |
| Dusk, with the own theme chosen | 2.82 | 2.8 | 2.43 (white behind) |
| Snowday, with the own theme chosen | 3.13 | 3.1 | 2.61 (black behind) |

(The Glassy card is the plate at 0.75 over the ground at 0.75: 0.75 of the card, 0.1875 of the ground and 0.0625 of the backdrop, as [Window opacity and corners](#window-opacity-and-corners) works it; the text is opaque and unchanged.) The loss is 15 per cent at most, and the look doesn't depend on the contrast alone: the outlines, the knobs and the frame are as opaque as ever.

**What it gives up, plainly.**
- Every disabled label is under 4.5:1: 2.8 to 3.1:1 on the card in the own themes and, with Omarchy, 2.3 to 4.0 by the theme's own range. WCAG exempts it. A person with low vision may find the section harder to read than the live ones: that is what disabled means, and the line under the switch says why.
- An unpressed button's outline is 1.2 to 1.4:1, which is almost nothing; the three words read as text. The frame of the saved value is the one mark that says "button" in the group. The dial below makes the hairline stronger.
- The saved value is told by a thin frame, 1 px, not a patch: it is found by looking for it, which the live pressed button, with its accent border, a fill and a bright label, is not.
- A disabled Snowday tile's caption is 2.3:1 on its well (it was 4.8).
- The Start at boot switch is grey while a flip runs, where it was bright, and is the change a person will notice first.

**What the owner should look at, in a real window, and the dials** (the designer didn't run the app; the dial figures are hand computations to a tenth):
1. **Does the section read as off at a glance, in the owner's own Omarchy theme?** It is the case the figures can't settle: the look is derived from the theme's own text and card. If it is still too strong: the shares become 0.35 and 0.50 (the text on the card 2.5:1 in Dusk and 2.7 in Snowday); if too faint: 0.45 and 0.60 (3.1 and 3.5). A share is one constant in `theme.rs` and `--a-disabled` in the mockups.
