- **The service not running comes first.** Without the service, the app can't tell whether this session may use OpenDeepCool, so it shows the service state, which holds no settings and no picture. In a session logind reports as remote or inactive, that state, the off screen, has no Start at boot switch ([Who may flip it](#who-may-flip-it)).
- **An administrator's own polkit rule** might allow reading but refuse changes. That's no separate state: the app shows the normal screens, and each change fails with `NotAuthorized`, shown like any [refused change](#refused-changes).

## Talking to the service

The D-Bus system bus (settings.md, Status). Only the user of the active local session can use OpenDeepCool: the service refuses every other session each read, watch and change (polkit `allow_active` only; owner, 2026-10-26). It offers no D-Bus properties, and nothing is broadcast.

- **Connecting:** the app starts watching the state (`Watch()`) and reads all of it (`GetState()`). The plan fixes the order, so that no change falls between the two. Then the window shows:
  - the [off screen](#the-off-screen), when the bus name has no owner;
  - [not available in this session](#not-available-in-this-session), when the service refuses with `NotAuthorized`;
  - otherwise, the normal screens with that state.
- **Connecting again,** one call at a time and never polling: whenever the bus name gets a new owner (`NameOwnerChanged`), and whenever the window becomes active (`notify::is-active`). This also renews any watch the service dropped meanwhile, for example while this session was inactive.
- **Updates** come as `StateChanged` signals addressed to this app alone, and preview frames the same way after `WatchFrames()`, only while the window is mapped ([Preview column](#preview-column)). The tiles' two inputs come with the same watch, and only to a caller that watches frames ([`dbus.md`](../spec/dbus.md)): the `Readings` key of `StateChanged`, at most once a second, and the `Levels` signal, once per service tick while a Music mode is shown and sound is attached ([Live tiles](#live-tiles)).
- **State names:** `CoolerState`, `AudioConnected`, `UnavailableReasons`, `Gpus` and `ChosenGpu` in this document are the names in the Phase 7–9 contract, and `GpuInUse` is the Phase 10 plan's (Task 2). They come in `GetState()` and `StateChanged`, not as properties, and the Phase 10 plan sets their final shape.
  - `UseSystemTheme` and `DarkTheme` (both `b`, defaults `true`) are Phase 11a Task 10's, with the calls `SetUseSystemTheme(b)` and `SetDarkTheme(b)` (`change-settings`; `docs/spec/dbus.md`). The Theme card reads and sets them, and the theme is resolved from them with the two inputs only the app has ([Which theme is used](#which-theme-is-used)). The service stores them and never interprets a theme.
  - `WindowOpacity` and `WindowCorners` (both `s`: `opaque`, `transparent` or `glassy`, default `opaque`; `sharp` or `rounded`, default `rounded`) are Phase 11a Task 49's (plan D11, owner 2026-11-22), with the calls `SetWindowOpacity(s)` and `SetWindowCorners(s)` (`change-settings`). The Default Theme group reads and sets them, and the window's own look is resolved from them and from `UseSystemTheme` ([Window opacity and corners](#window-opacity-and-corners)). The service stores them and never interprets them; an unknown word is refused with `InvalidArgs`, as the plan says.
  - The cooler's part of the window follows `CoolerState` (`connected`, `not-found`, `no-access`, `write-failed`; [Banners](#banners)). `CoolerConnected` stays in the state, but the app doesn't read it.
  - The GPU row reads `Gpus` (its kinds include `query-failed`), `ChosenGpu` (empty for Automatic) and `GpuInUse` ([Display](#display)).
  - The tiles read `Readings` (`a{s(xt)}`: each metric that has a value, by the spec's name, as the exact fraction the display rounds from; a metric with no value is left out), `AudioConnected`, `Mode`, `MusicPeaks`, `TemperatureUnit` and `UnavailableReasons`, and the `Levels` signal (`ad`, 14 values from 0 to 1). The app draws every tile from them with `odc_render`, reads no sensor and no sound, and sends the service nothing for the tiles ([Live tiles](#live-tiles)). A `Readings` that arrives with `GetState()` is the first answer; an empty one is "no value", not "no answer yet".
- **Start at boot** reads systemd, not the service, so it works while the service is down ([Where its state comes from](#where-its-state-comes-from)).
- **Back from standby** (owner, 2026-10-26): no screen of its own, and nothing for the user to do. While the monitor is off or the screen is locked, the session stays active, so nothing changes for the app. After a sleep, the service takes up the cooler again by itself (PRD, Service model), and the preview follows the cooler as soon as frames come back. If the cooler or the sound takes a moment to return, the cooler banner and the sound status show it as usual.

## States

| State | What the app shows | Mockup |
|---|---|---|
| Normal, several GPUs | Header: the title in the middle, the gear and the close icon at the right. Display page: its page bar ("Display", the Refresh icon at the right) and the tile gallery. The preview column: the picture, the caption ("Temperature · CPU + GPU") and the status items "Cooler connected" and "Service running", with no line between the caption and the status. Configuration (the gear pressed): its page bar (the back icon, "Configuration"), then the Theme card (Use system theme, and the Default Theme group: Appearance, Window Opacity, Window Corners), Rotation, Temperature unit and the GPU on Automatic with "Using Dedicated · Contoso Graphics R870." under it, each label beside its control and the controls in one column, and the three switches (Use system theme, Peak dots, Start at boot) at 32 × 18 | [display.html](mockups/display.html), [configuration.html](mockups/configuration.html) |
| The Configuration page is open | The gear is pressed; the back icon, Escape or Alt+Left, or the gear again, return to the Display page, and focus goes to the gear | [configuration.html](mockups/configuration.html): click the back icon, the gear, or press Escape |
| The temperature unit | A row of the Display card after Rotation; the temperature tiles on the Display page follow it | [configuration.html](mockups/configuration.html), [display-scrolling.html](mockups/display-scrolling.html) (the tiles show °F) |
| GPU list open | Automatic first; a GPU whose kind query failed as "Unknown type · …" with "driver couldn't be asked"; a GPU without a reading rule last, with "no readings"; the line under the dropdown names the integrated GPU, which Automatic reads here | [configuration-gpu-list.html](mockups/configuration-gpu-list.html) |
| A GPU added or removed since the last start | The choice is back on Automatic (the service cleared it), with no note; the line names the GPU in use | [configuration.html](mockups/configuration.html) |
| A Music mode, sound attached | The status line adds "Sound connected". The Music tiles and the Dancer are live: their spectrum follows the same levels as the preview's, and the Dancer steps with them | [display-music.html](mockups/display-music.html) |
| A Music mode, no sound attached | The status line adds "No sound: spectrum is silent"; the preview shows the silent spectrum (one bar row) and the Music tiles show their still sample: they are live only with sound attached | [display-music-no-sound.html](mockups/display-music-no-sound.html) |
| A mode outside Music (sound attached or not) | The Music tiles, the emoji and the Dancer included, show the still sample and the emoji their first picture; every other tile moves as the cooler's picture would | [display.html](mockups/display.html) |
| A value of 100 or more, °F | The preview mirrors the scrolling strip; every tile whose value is 100 or more scrolls too, together; the temperature tiles show °F | [display-scrolling.html](mockups/display-scrolling.html) |
| A tile whose metric has no value | Dashes (`--%`, `--°C`), `--disabled` on an unavailable tile, `--fg` on the saved mode's; the other value of a pair a number | [unavailable-metric.html](mockups/unavailable-metric.html), [reading-lost.html](mockups/reading-lost.html) |
| The tiles are off screen (the Configuration page, a hidden, minimised or suspended window, the off screen) | No tick, no redraw, no `Levels` used; on coming back every tile starts again at home, together | — ([Live tiles](#when-the-tick-runs)) |
| Reduced motion (the system's preference, or animations off) | The tiles stand still at rest, drawn from the current readings; nothing in the window says so; the preview keeps moving | — ([Live tiles](#reduced-motion)) |
| First start | Load · CPU, 0°, °C, peak dots on, Moderate / Slow, the GPU on Automatic | [first-start.html](mockups/first-start.html) |
| Cooler disconnected (`not-found`) | Cooler banner; status "Cooler not connected"; the preview's description adds "Not on the cooler: it isn't connected."; the column has the same lines as when connected; everything still changeable | [cooler-disconnected.html](mockups/cooler-disconnected.html) |
| Cooler found, no access (`no-access`) | Error banner with the fix; status "Cooler: no access"; the preview's description adds "Not on the cooler: the service can't open it."; everything still changeable | [cooler-no-access.html](mockups/cooler-no-access.html) |
| Sending to the cooler fails, 2 s or more (`write-failed`) | Warning banner; status "Cooler: sending fails"; the preview's description adds "Not on the cooler: sending it fails."; everything still changeable | [cooler-write-failed.html](mockups/cooler-write-failed.html) |
| Sending fails, under 2 s (one bad write, or the cooler being unplugged) | As connected: no banner, until the state changes or 2 s pass | (as display) |
| Service not running, set to start at boot (stopped, failed or restarting) | The off screen headed "OpenDeepCool isn't running", status "Service not running" (red); no banner; the switch off, with "Not fully on: the display service failed. Turn it on to finish. The journal says why the service failed."; the header keeps the title and the close icon, with the gear visible but insensitive, and there's no Refresh (it belongs to the Display page); nothing else changeable | [service-not-running.html](mockups/service-not-running.html) |

(and, with no preference, "Using OpenDeepCool's dark theme. Your desktop has no preference."). The window follows the three | [configuration-theme-own.html](mockups/configuration-theme-own.html) |

## Theme

### Which theme is used

The theme is one of three sources, resolved from four inputs, by one pure function that the Theme card, the stylesheet and the tests all use. **Only Omarchy's themes are followed** (owner, 2026-11-23: "Use system theme" supports only Omarchy for now, said in the README and in `docs/spec/settings.md`): no other desktop's colour theme is read, and the second source below follows nothing but the desktop's dark or light preference:

1. **The Omarchy theme**, as in Phase 10, when one is found and the system theme is followed;
2. **OpenDeepCool's own theme, dark or light, from the system's preference**, when no Omarchy theme is found, the system theme is followed, and GTK reports a preference;
3. **OpenDeepCool's own theme, dark or light, as chosen**, when the system theme isn't followed, or no system theme can be found: `DarkTheme` decides.

**The inputs.**
- **An Omarchy theme found:** `colors.toml` of the current theme was read and parses as TOML. A TOML file with keys missing counts as found, and each missing key takes the own theme's value of the same scheme, as before; a file that isn't TOML counts as not found, and the journal says why (`theme.rs` already used the default theme for it).
- **GTK's preference:** `GtkSettings:gtk-interface-color-scheme`, which GDK fills from the XDG portal and which sends `notify` when the system changes it (Phase 11a Task 1): `UNSUPPORTED` (the system gives none), `DEFAULT` (no preference), `DARK`, `LIGHT`.
- **`UseSystemTheme`**, **`DarkTheme`**, **`WindowOpacity`** and **`WindowCorners`**, the service's four settings (`GetState` and `StateChanged`); with an Omarchy theme found, also **Omarchy's Window Opacity**, read from its two toggle files ([Window opacity and corners](#window-opacity-and-corners), section 6). The two window settings change the window only when the system theme isn't followed; the first two decide that.

**The derived facts.** `found` = an Omarchy theme is found, or the preference isn't `UNSUPPORTED`. `follow` = `UseSystemTheme` and `found`. The dark or light control is sensitive exactly when `follow` is false; the switch is sensitive exactly when `found` is true and shows `follow`.

| Omarchy theme found | GTK preference | `UseSystemTheme` | `DarkTheme` | The theme | Use system theme | Appearance shows | The card's line |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| yes | any | on | any | the Omarchy theme | on, sensitive | insensitive; its `mode` | "Using the Omarchy theme." |
| yes | any | off | on | own, dark | off, sensitive | sensitive; Dark | "Using OpenDeepCool's dark theme." |
| yes | any | off | off | own, light | off, sensitive | sensitive; Light | "Using OpenDeepCool's light theme." |
| no | `DARK` | on | any | own, dark | on, sensitive | insensitive; Dark | "Using OpenDeepCool's dark theme, as your desktop prefers." |
| no | `LIGHT` | on | any | own, light | on, sensitive | insensitive; Light | "Using OpenDeepCool's light theme, as your desktop prefers." |
| no | `DEFAULT` | on | any | own, dark | on, sensitive | insensitive; Dark | "Using OpenDeepCool's dark theme. Your desktop has no preference." |
| no | `DARK`, `LIGHT` or `DEFAULT` | off | on | own, dark | off, sensitive | sensitive; Dark | "Using OpenDeepCool's dark theme." |
| no | `DARK`, `LIGHT` or `DEFAULT` | off | off | own, light | off, sensitive | sensitive; Light | "Using OpenDeepCool's light theme." |
| no | `UNSUPPORTED` | on | on | own, dark | **off, insensitive** | sensitive; Dark | the warning: "OpenDeepCool can't find the system's theme and is using its own." |
| no | `UNSUPPORTED` | on | off | own, light | **off, insensitive** | sensitive; Light | the warning |
| no | `UNSUPPORTED` | off | on | own, dark | **off, insensitive** | sensitive; Dark | the warning |
| no | `UNSUPPORTED` | off | off | own, light | **off, insensitive** | sensitive; Light | the warning |

The 12 rows cover all 32 combinations of the inputs (2 × 4 × 2 × 2): 8 + 4 + 4 + 2 + 2 + 2 + 3 + 3 + 4. The tests assert all 32.

- **"Its `mode`"** is the Omarchy theme's `colors.toml` `mode`, `dark` or `light`; `dark` when missing or invalid, as `theme.rs` does. It is also the scheme the stylesheet's base takes ([How it's applied](#how-its-applied)).
- **An Omarchy theme found wins over the preference** while the system theme is followed (Phase 11a Task 1's rule), so the first row has no preference column.
- **`DEFAULT` shows OpenDeepCool's dark theme:** no preference is "dark first", this PC's desktop.
- **The service's value is never rewritten by the app.** In the warning rows the switch is disabled and off though the service may hold `UseSystemTheme` true; nothing is sent, and the switch turns on by itself when a system theme appears.
- **Window Opacity and Window Corners follow the Appearance column's sensitivity** (Tasks 50 and 51): the whole Default Theme group, its heading, its three titles and every button, is disabled (in the disabled look, D55) exactly when `follow` is true, and enabled otherwise. The same `follow` decides whether the two window settings are applied: they are, only when the theme is OpenDeepCool's own and `follow` is false, rows 2, 3, 7, 8 and 9 to 12 of the table; with an Omarchy theme followed the window takes Omarchy's opacity, and with the own theme followed from the preference it is opaque and rounded ([Window opacity and corners](#window-opacity-and-corners)). The tests assert the 32 combinations again for the group's sensitivity.

### How it's applied

- **Three sources** (above): the app resolves them whenever one of the four inputs changes, then builds the one provider's CSS from the result.
- **One provider.**
  - A single `GtkCssProvider` is added with `gtk_style_context_add_provider_for_display` at `GTK_STYLE_PROVIDER_PRIORITY_APPLICATION` (600). Whether that function is deprecated in current GTK is **unverified**; the plan checks.
  - A user's own `~/.config/gtk-4.0/gtk.css` keeps the last word at USER priority, 800 (verified).
- **Generated CSS.**
  - The CSS is a string: a `:root { … }` block of custom properties, followed by the app's fixed stylesheet, which uses only `var(--…)`. It loads with `gtk_css_provider_load_from_string`.
  - GTK supports `:root`, custom properties and `var()` since 4.16 (verified, GTK CSS Properties). Arch's repository ships a newer GTK.
- **Colours with alpha** (`rgba(…)`) are computed in Rust and written into the `:root` block as literals, so the stylesheet needs no CSS colour functions.
- **Live reload (Omarchy).**
  - A `GFileMonitor` on `~/.local/state/omarchy/current/` reacts when `theme` is replaced (the PRD, Theme: themes are moved over `current/theme`; Omarchy stages the new one in `current/next-theme`, removes `current/theme` and moves it in, its `omarchy-theme-set`, read 2026-11-06). A second one on `current/theme/` catches an edit in place, and while a folder of that path is missing, the nearest existing folder above it is watched, so a theme that appears later is found (decisions log D65).
  - After 150 ms of quiet, the app re-reads `colors.toml` and `shell.toml` and, when the tokens changed, regenerates the string, reloads the same provider, and repaints the preview and tiles. There's no restart, and the page and scroll position are kept. The source at start and each reload are logged (`theme: the Omarchy theme`, `theme: OpenDeepCool's dark theme`, `theme: reloaded, …`), with each missing or rejected key.
- **The other inputs are live too.** `notify::gtk-interface-color-scheme` on `GtkSettings` (measured live by Phase 11a Task 1) and the service's `StateChanged` for `UseSystemTheme`, `DarkTheme`, `WindowOpacity` and `WindowCorners` resolve the theme again and reload the same provider, in place, like an Omarchy reload: no restart, the same page and scroll position, the preview and tiles repainted. The change is a cut, with no cross-fade.
- **Fallback rules.**
  - When `colors.toml` is missing or isn't TOML, no Omarchy theme is found: the theme is OpenDeepCool's own by [the table](#which-theme-is-used), and the journal names the problem.
  - When one key is missing or invalid, that token comes from OpenDeepCool's own theme of the same scheme (dark, or light when `mode` says light), and the journal names the key.
- **The window's own look** (owner, 2026-11-22; it **supersedes** the first design's "no window opacity and no blur, ever: Hyprland applies them", which the owner's request reversed): the one provider also carries the window's ground and cards with the opacity of the theme in use, the blur request of Glassy and the corners of the own theme. The inputs are two more, the mode of Omarchy's Window Opacity, read from two toggle files and watched as the theme is ([Window opacity and corners](#window-opacity-and-corners)), and the service's `WindowOpacity` and `WindowCorners` (`StateChanged`). The app still writes no compositor rule, and never touches Hyprland's own opacity, blur or rounding.
- **No motion:** the stylesheet sets `transition: none` and no CSS animations, overriding GTK's built-in theme. The global `gtk-enable-animations` setting is left alone, because it belongs to the user: the app never writes it (nor `gtk-interface-reduced-motion`), and reads both, for one purpose, to keep the live tiles at rest where the user has asked for less motion ([Reduced motion](#reduced-motion)).
- **Light or dark base.** The resolved theme's scheme (an Omarchy theme's `mode`, or OpenDeepCool's own dark or light) sets the provider's `prefers-color-scheme`, so parts the stylesheet doesn't colour (scrollbars, popover arrows) match; `gtk-application-prefer-dark-theme` is deprecated since GTK 4.20 ([Unverified points, answered (Phase 10 Task 1, 2026-11-06)](#unverified-points-answered-phase-10-task-1-2026-11-06)).
- **Font:** the GTK system font (`gtk-font-name`), at the sizes of [Type](#type). `shell.toml` `[font]` isn't read in v1.
- **Room for later:** a "theme source" setting after v1 (following another desktop's theme) only swaps where the tokens come from. The stylesheet and the token names stay. Nothing about it shows in v1; the Theme card chooses only between following the system and OpenDeepCool's own theme.

### Tokens and where they come from

| Token | Omarchy source | Used for |
|---|---|---|
| `--bg` | `colors.toml` `background` | the ground's **opaque tone**: what the contrast table is computed against, the ring of a knob, the base of a banner's tint. It is what the window paints only through `--ground` |
| `--surface` | `lighter_background` | the opaque tone of a card; the preview frame, popovers, tooltips and the feedback label, which are always opaque; the cards paint it through `--plate` |
| `--ground` | computed: `--bg` at the window's opacity | **new in Task 45:** the one background the window paints, which the header bar, the preview column, the pages and the off screen show through. Opaque (alpha 1) in opaque mode |
| `--plate` | computed: `--surface` at the window's opacity | **new in Task 45:** the cards' background, painted over the ground; **and, since Task 61, a tile's, in OpenDeepCool's own light theme only** (the window class `own-light`: [Answers decided on 2026-11-24](#answers-decided-on-2026-11-24-tasks-60-and-61)) |
| `--none` | the colour `transparent`, written in `:root` | **new in Task 45:** the resting fill of the frameless icon buttons and of links, in place of `--bg`: a fill of `--bg` over a see-through ground would show as a box. The test that forbids a colour literal outside `:root` forbids `transparent` too, which is why the token exists |
| `--fg` | `foreground` | text, lit preview dots (unlit dots: `--fg` at 0.12) |
| `--muted` | `muted` | hints, notes, group reason lines: secondary text that is live. **Not** what a disabled control or a disabled heading uses (`--disabled`, since Task 63). With an Omarchy theme this is the theme's own key, whose contrast on the card varies from theme to theme (1.3 to 1.6:1 in the five shipped themes worked by hand, higher in others: [Answers decided on 2026-11-24, Task 63](#answers-decided-on-2026-11-24-task-63)), which is one reason the disabled colour is derived from `--fg` and `--surface` and not from it |
| `--disabled` | **computed** (Task 63, D55): `--fg` over `--surface`, opaque, by 40 per cent in a dark theme and 55 per cent in a light one (`Tokens::disabled()`; an Omarchy theme takes its own `foreground`, `lighter_background` and `mode`, so it has no key of its own and nothing to log as missing) | the text, icon, knob and picture dots of a disabled control; a chosen toggle's frame and an on switch's trough while disabled: the one disabled look ([Theme card](#theme-card), The look). Dusk `#798491`, Snowday `#80848c`. Never alpha, so its contrast is a known pair against the card |
| `--line` | computed: `--fg` at 0.12 | (already there; listed here because Task 63 uses it as the disabled border) |
| `--accent` | `accent` | selected tile and toggle border, focus ring, switch on, scale fill, links |
| `--red`, `--yellow`, `--green` | `red`, `yellow`, `green` | status dots, banner icons and tints, warning text |
| `--selection` | `selection` | selected text in the selectable command label |
| `--ctl-normal-fill`, `--ctl-normal-border`, `--ctl-normal-border-width` | `shell.toml` `[controls]` `normal-color` + `normal-fill-alpha`, `normal-border` + `normal-border-alpha`, `normal-border-width` | idle buttons, toggles, dropdown buttons, tiles |
| `--ctl-hover-fill`, `--ctl-hover-border` | `hover-cursor-*` | `:hover` |
| `--ctl-focus-*` | `focus-*` | `:focus-visible` fill and border |
| `--ctl-selected-fill`, `--ctl-selected-border` | `selected-*` | `:checked` toggles, the selected tile (not in OpenDeepCool's own light theme, where the chosen tile keeps its card fill and is framed in `--accent`), the selected list item |
| `--ctl-pressed-fill` | `pressed-fill-alpha` | `:active` |
| `--radius` | (none in `shell.toml`) | controls: buttons, toggles, tiles, the dropdown button, the icon buttons, the command box. 0 px with an Omarchy theme, 4 px in OpenDeepCool's own while Window Corners is rounded, 0 while it is sharp |
| `--radius-box` | (none in `shell.toml`) | what holds controls: cards, the preview frame, popovers, the feedback label. 0 px with an Omarchy theme, 10 px in OpenDeepCool's own while Window Corners is rounded, 0 while it is sharp. **New in Task 11** |
| `--radius-win` | (none) | **new in Task 51:** the window's own corners (the client-side decoration). 12 px in OpenDeepCool's own theme while rounded, 0 while sharp; **none with an Omarchy theme**: the rule that uses it is under the class `own-corners`, which the app sets only for the own theme, so the window keeps what it has today |
| `--radius-pill` | (none) | **new in Task 51:** a switch's trough and a scale's bar, a third of it (the bar's 3 px against the trough's 9). 9 px in every theme but a sharp own one, where it is 0 |
| `--radius-knob` | (none) | **new in Task 51:** a switch's and a scale's knob. `100%` (round) in every theme but a sharp own one, where it is 0 |
| `--radius-bar` | (none) | **as built:** a scale's bar, a third of `--radius-pill` (3 px, or 0), a token of its own, so the stylesheet needs no `calc()` (whether GTK's CSS takes one on a variable is unverified) |

- **Computed tokens**, from the fixed alphas of the mockups: `--line` (`--fg` at 0.12: header, column and banner rules), `--unlit` (`--fg` at 0.12: unlit preview dots; the display's own alpha, never the window's), `--banner-error` and `--banner-warn` (`--red` at 0.16 and `--yellow` at 0.14 over `--bg`, then at the window's opacity like the ground: opaque tints in opaque mode), and, from Task 45, `--ground` and `--plate`, which are `--bg` and `--surface` at the window's opacity (1, 0.985 or 0.75, [Window opacity and corners](#window-opacity-and-corners)), and, from Task 63, `--disabled`, `--fg` over `--surface` by 0.40 (a dark theme) or 0.55 (a light one), rounded per channel like `blend`, **opaque** like the text colours it stands for (`:root` then holds 33 tokens). The rule's inputs are the mode and the tone, so the numbers are computed in Rust and written into `:root` as literals, like every colour with an alpha.
- **Values the tables leave open** (decisions log D30–D32, D39, D43):
  - a border width is CSS's four sides, as `shell.toml`'s comment allows (`N`, `"Y X"`, `"T X B"`, `"T R B L"`, with `-top`, `-right`, `-bottom` and `-left` keys over it), each from 0 to 16 px;
  - a missing or rejected `shell.toml` colour key takes the theme's own `--fg`, as Omarchy's template writes them, and a missing alpha the default theme's; `--ctl-pressed-fill` uses `normal-color`;
  - OpenDeepCool's own theme's focus fill and border are its hover ones, its selected border is `--fg` opaque (the stylesheet draws a selected tile and toggle with `--accent`), its border widths 1 px, and its `--selection` is `--accent` at **0.25** over `--bg` (Task 11's lowered alpha, which the faceplate's light-tone accent needed for `--fg` text on the selection to reach 4.5:1; kept in Tasks 44, 54, 59, 61 and 62, where it has room: row 15 of [the contrast table](#the-contrast-table) is 6.6 and 6.2). A missing `selection` key of an Omarchy theme takes that theme's own `accent` at 0.35 over its own `background`, never the own theme's colours;
  - a `colors.toml` colour's own alpha is dropped, so the window's opacity is the mode's alone (Omarchy's Window Opacity, or the own theme's setting); a `shell.toml` colour's own alpha is multiplied by its `*-alpha` key;
  - a disabled control has `--disabled` text, the hairline `--line` for a border and the normal fill, since the CSS has no `opacity` (Task 63, D55; D43's muted text on the normal fill and border is history), and what is chosen or on keeps a frame or a filled trough in `--disabled`: [Theme card](#theme-card), The look.
- **Focus ring:** keyboard focus also gets a 2 px `--accent` outline, whatever the theme's focus alphas, so focus is always visible (WCAG 2.4.7).
- **Gradients:** `shell.toml` border keys may hold a Hyprland-style gradient (`"rgba(…) rgba(…) 45deg"`, per its comments). The app uses the gradient's first colour, since a gradient border colour in GTK CSS is **unverified**.

### Window opacity and corners

**What the owner asked** (2026-11-22): with the system theme, the window follows Omarchy's Window Opacity (opaque, transparent, glassy); with OpenDeepCool's own theme there are the same three, labelled **Window Opacity**, and **Window Corners**, sharp or rounded, as settings of the service. This **supersedes** the first design's rule that the window's opacity and blur are the compositor's and the app never sets them (PRD, Technical Approach, Theme; the designer brief). The owner's request is the later decision, and the PRD's sentence is for the planner to bring in line.

**What Omarchy does, read on 2026-11-22** in its own files (`/usr/share/omarchy`, `/usr/bin/omarchy-hyprland-window-transparency` and `omarchy-hyprland-corners`):
- **Window Opacity** is `opaque`, `transparent` (the default) or `glassy`. The programs keep it as toggle files in `${XDG_STATE_HOME:-~/.local/state}/omarchy/toggles/hypr/`: `window-opaque.lua` and `window-glassy.lua`. `current_mode()` checks the opaque file first, then the glassy file, and answers transparent when neither exists, so **both files present means opaque**. The programs write the new file before removing the old one, so a moment with both is normal.
- **What each mode does to a window.** *Opaque* sets the opacity of every window that Omarchy makes see-through (the tag `transparent-window`) to `1 1`. *Transparent* leaves those windows at Omarchy's standard opacity, **0.985 focused and 0.96 unfocused** (`helpers.lua`, `transparent_window`), and Hyprland's blur is off (`looknfeel.lua`). *Glassy* turns Hyprland's blur on for see-through windows (`glass.lua`: size 4, 2 passes), gives terminals a see-through background (0.65, according to the Phase 11a plan; `omarchy-theme-set-glass` itself wasn't opened), and, for **GTK apps**, fades Nautilus as a whole (`opacity 0.75 0.75`, "since GTK draws their backgrounds solid") and relies on the `backdrop-filter` that `default/gtk/glass.css` adds to GTK apps: `window { backdrop-filter: blur(20px); }`.
- **Which windows are see-through at all** (`windows.lua`): terminals and a short list of desktop apps (Nautilus, 1Password, omawrite) opt in. Another GTK app is solid in every mode unless the user tags it. OpenDeepCool follows the mode **as if it were one of the opted-in windows**, which is what the owner asked; it can't opt itself in with a Hyprland rule (rule 14), so it draws the alpha itself.
- **Window Corners** is `theme`, `sharp` or `rounded`: sharp is rounding 0, rounded is rounding 6 with power 3, drawn by the compositor. The app doesn't read it.

**1. Which surfaces carry the alpha.** The app paints its window background and its cards with the mode's alpha, so a see-through window shows the backdrop through the ground and, a little less, through the cards. The map, for the stylesheet:

| Surface | Painted with | Why |
|---|---|---|
| The window (`window`, `window.background`), which the header bar, the preview column, the pages, the scrolled windows and the off screen show through | `--ground` | Painted **once**. None of its children paints a ground of its own: the header bar's `background-color` becomes `--none`, or the alpha would compound. |
| A card (Theme, Display, Music, Startup) | `--plate`, over the ground | A card is denser than the ground, since it paints over it: its alpha is 1 − (1 − α)², so 0.94 in Glassy. The page's text is in the cards. |
| A tile, **in OpenDeepCool's own light theme only** (Task 61) | `--plate`, as a card; an unavailable tile `--ctl-normal-fill` | A tile is a card there, so it is as dense as one. With an Omarchy theme or the dark own theme a tile is the control's normal fill, a tint over the ground, as before. |
| A banner | `--banner-error` or `--banner-warn`, the tint at the window's opacity | The tint is the ground tinted, so it is see-through as the ground is. |
| The frameless icon buttons and links at rest, and their disabled state | `--none` | A fill of `--bg` over a see-through ground would show as a box. |
| **Opaque in every mode:** the preview's frame | `--surface` | It is the picture's plate: the dots and their contrast (row 13 of the table) are drawn on a known tone. |
| **Opaque in every mode:** the open GPU list, tooltips, the "Refreshed" and refusal notice | `--surface` | They stand over text, and a see-through list over text can't be read. |
| **Never alpha:** text, icons, the picture's dots (the unlit ones keep their own alpha, the display's, `--unlit`), the status dots, the focus ring, knobs and their ring, a control's border | their own tokens | They carry the information. The controls' fills (`--ctl-*`) are tints over whatever is under them and don't change. |

**2. The alpha numbers.**

| Mode | Ground's alpha | A card, over the ground | Blur | Where the number comes from |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Opaque | 1.00 | 1.00 | none | Omarchy's `opacity "1 1"` |
| Transparent | **0.985** | 0.9998 | none | Omarchy's standard opacity for a see-through window, focused. Not followed: its unfocused 0.96 (2.5 points no one can see, for twice the tokens and a `:backdrop` rule) |
| Glassy | **0.75** | 0.94 | `backdrop-filter: blur(20px)` on the window, as Omarchy's own `glass.css` | Omarchy's `0.75 0.75` for Nautilus, the one GTK app it makes see-through. The 20 px is its own; the compositor's blur settings decide how it looks |

Transparent is subtle by Omarchy's own design: at 0.985 a window shows 1.5 per cent of its backdrop, which no eye picks out. Omarchy's own transparent windows look the same, so a user who flips between Use system theme and the own theme sees the same window for the same word. If the owner wants Transparent to be visibly see-through in the own theme, the one number to raise is this row's, at the price of no longer matching Omarchy.

**3. Contrast for a see-through window.** WCAG's ratio is defined for two known colours, and behind a see-through window one of them is the user's backdrop, which no design can know. The rule of this design:
- Every figure in [the contrast table](#the-contrast-table) is computed against the opaque tones, alpha 1. It is the guarantee of the opaque mode, which is the own theme's default.
- The loss in the other two modes is bounded, because the lowest alpha is 0.75: the backdrop is at most a quarter of the ground. For the worst backdrop, a uniform white under the dark theme and a uniform black under the light one, at Glassy (hand computations, good to about a tenth):

  | Pair | Dark theme, white behind | Light theme, black behind |
  |---|---|---|
  | text on the bare ground | 4.9 | 4.8 |
  | text on a card | 7.7 | 9.2 |
  | muted text on a card | **4.6** | 5.4 |
  | accent on a card | 4.4 | 5.3 |
  | muted text, or accent, on the bare ground | 2.9, 2.8 | 2.9, 2.8 |

  (Redone for Cool Blue Forest on 2026-11-23, from the colours as blended in sRGB over the backdrop: the ground at 0.75 of itself plus 0.25 of the backdrop, a card at 0.75 of itself plus 0.1875 of the ground and 0.0625 of the backdrop. **The light column was redone on 2026-11-24 for Snowday's toned-down ground and card of Task 62** (Task 61's lighter Snowday gave 5.6, 10.5, 5.7, 6.1 and 3.0, 3.3; Task 59's 5.3, 10.2, 5.5, 5.9 and 2.8, 3.1); the dark column is unchanged. Meltwater's figures were 6.6 and 6.4, 10.0 and 12.0, 5.1 and 5.8, and 3.3, 4.3 against 3.1, 3.0; Coolant's before them. The dark theme loses most: its ground is the owner's mid-toned charcoal, CIE lightness about 20 where Meltwater's was 11, so a white backdrop lifts it further.)

- **So Glassy keeps 4.5:1 for `--fg` text everywhere and for muted text on the cards, though the dark theme's 4.6 is only 2 per cent over, and can't promise it for an accent link on a dark card (4.4), for muted text and an accent link on the bare ground, nor the 3:1 of a status dot (the red dot of the dark theme is about 2.0 over white, and the light theme's red and green dots about 2.4 and 2.0 over black), over a backdrop at the opposite extreme of the theme.** The bare ground carries the tile gallery's captions, the reason lines and the preview column. Each status has words or an icon, so nothing depends on colour alone. The blur makes a busy backdrop its average, so the worst case is a uniformly bright (or dark) wallpaper, and the user chose it: Omarchy fades all of Nautilus's content to 0.75.
- Transparent changes each figure by under 5 per cent (at most 1.5 per cent of the backdrop). Opaque is unchanged.

**4. OpenDeepCool's own theme: Window Opacity and Window Corners.** The service's `WindowOpacity` (`opaque`, `transparent`, `glassy`; default `opaque`) gives the alpha and the blur of the table above. The service's `WindowCorners` (`sharp`, `rounded`; default `rounded`) gives the radii:

| Part | Rounded | Sharp | Token |
|---|---|---|---|
| The window's own corners (GTK's client-side decoration) | 12 px | 0 | `--radius-win` |
| Cards, the preview's frame, the open list, the feedback notice | 10 px | 0 | `--radius-box` |
| Buttons, the toggles of a group (its two outer corners), tiles, the dropdown's button, the icon buttons | 4 px | 0 | `--radius` |
| A switch's trough; a scale's bar (a third of it, 3 px) | 9 px (a pill at 18 px) | 0 | `--radius-pill` |
| A switch's and a scale's knob | round | 0, square | `--radius-knob` |
| The status dots; the dots of the preview and of the tiles | round | round | fixed: they are the picture and its indicators |

Sharp is one rule with one exception, the dots, which are what the product draws. The three radii of the rounded look have three sizes by what the box is (the window 12, what holds controls 10, a control 4), not one on everything.

**5. What applies from where.** The same style is resolved from the source of the theme:

| Source ([Which theme is used](#which-theme-is-used)) | Ground's alpha and blur | Corners |
|---|---|---|
| An Omarchy theme, followed | **Omarchy's mode**, read live (1, 0.985, or 0.75 with the blur) | **Not touched** (owner, 2026-11-22). The compositor does the window's corners. The app writes no rule for the window's radius, so it stays whatever it is today (GTK's own for a client-side decoration: **unverified**, the plan says 12 px); the controls and boxes are 0 as before, and the switches and knobs are round as before. Window Corners is neither read nor applied |
| OpenDeepCool's own theme, from the system's preference | 1, no blur: **the saved Window Opacity isn't applied** | Rounded: the own theme's defaults, window 12 px. The saved Window Corners isn't applied |
| OpenDeepCool's own theme, as chosen (the system theme off, or none found) | the service's `WindowOpacity` | the service's `WindowCorners` |

**6. How Omarchy's mode reaches the app.** A pure function of the two files, `window_mode(opaque_exists, glassy_exists)`: opaque if the opaque file is a file, else glassy if the glassy file is a file, else transparent. A path that is a directory, unreadable or missing counts as absent. The app reads the two paths, never runs Omarchy's programs, watches `toggles/hypr/` with a `GFileMonitor` (the nearest existing folder above it while it is missing, as the theme's watch does, [How it's applied](#how-its-applied)) and re-reads after 150 ms of quiet, which also covers the program's "write the new, then remove the old" moment. It reads the files only while an Omarchy theme is found.

**7. What a change does.** A new mode, or a confirmed `WindowOpacity` or `WindowCorners`, regenerates the same provider: a new `--ground`, `--plate`, `--banner-*` and radii, and the window's classes `glassy` (the blur), `own-corners` (the window's radius rule, which exists only for the own theme) and `own-light` (the tile and chosen-toggle rules of OpenDeepCool's own light theme, set with the theme and not by the window mode: [Answers decided on 2026-11-24](#answers-decided-on-2026-11-24-tasks-60-and-61)). A change is a cut, in place, with the page and its scroll position kept.

**8. Not verified here** (the plan's Task 45 measures each, and the report says what it found):
- That the app's pinned GTK accepts `backdrop-filter: blur(...)` without a CSS warning, and what it does: Omarchy's `glass.css` uses it for GTK apps, and GTK 4.24's release notes mention Wayland's background-effect protocol (Phoronix, read 2026-11-22 as a search summary), but the property's page in the CSS reference wasn't opened.
- That the window is see-through at all in GTK's client-side decoration under weston, and with the app's `box-shadow: none`.
- What a compositor that draws no blur does with Glassy: the window is see-through and sharp, with no blur.
- A rounded window (12 px) in a compositor that draws a square border around it, such as Hyprland with rounding 0: the border shows outside the curve. That is what Sharp is for, and the reason the corners of an Omarchy theme aren't touched.
- The radius GTK's stock client-side decoration gives this window today.

**9. What the tests check** (Tasks 45 and 51):
- **The pure rules, with no display:** `window_mode` for the four combinations of the two files, with a directory, an unreadable path and a missing one counting as absent (both present is opaque); and the resolved style, a pure function of the source, `follow`, Omarchy's mode, `WindowOpacity` and `WindowCorners`, for all of them: the ground's alpha, the blur, and the five radii. The table of section 5 is its specification.
- **Sampled pixels in a headless weston** (`--features weston-tests`): the window's alpha is the mode's in each of the three modes (and 1 for the own theme followed from the preference); a card's is denser; the preview's frame and its lit dot, the open list and the notice are opaque; text is opaque; the window's corner pixels follow the radii in each of the 12 combinations of the own theme.
- **Live:** creating or removing a toggle file changes the window while an Omarchy theme is followed; a confirmed `WindowOpacity` or `WindowCorners` changes it while the own theme is chosen and changes nothing while the system theme is followed.

### OpenDeepCool's own theme

Designed in Phase 11a Task 11 (owner, 2026-11-18: "It's us that defines the default OpenDeepCool theme") as a monochrome faceplate, **redesigned in Task 44** (owner, 2026-11-22: "improve OpenDeepCool Dark and Light default theme (not the system). Its ugly right now, just white and black. You can do much better. Use the Anthropic Frontend Design to create a much better Dark and Light themes.") as Coolant, a petrol blue with a rose accent and a warm cream text, **redesigned again in Task 54** (owner, 2026-11-23: "still terrible", and the theme should fit what the app configures, a water cooler: an ocean and ice palette, dark and light) as Meltwater, and **a third time in Task 59** (owner, 2026-11-23: "the default app theme is still very bad. Please use this color scheme and create the dark and light versions") as **Cool Blue Forest**, below: the owner's own colour scheme, Folk & Fey's "Cool Blue Forest" from its Winter Forest Collection, in two lights, **Dusk** (dark) and **Snowday** (light). **Snowday was redone in Task 61** (owner, 2026-11-24: "the app theme in light mod is still bad... take a photo and look at it"): a lighter and bluer ground, tiles that are cards, and a frame for what is chosen ([D53](#decisions); [Answers decided on 2026-11-24](#answers-decided-on-2026-11-24-tasks-60-and-61)); its first values, Task 59's, are history. **It was toned down the same day in Task 62** (owner, 2026-11-24: "the light theme is too bright now"): a ground at L\* 84 and a card at L\* 93 in place of Task 61's 90 and 98, with the hint and two status colours a little deeper and the structure of Task 61 kept ([D54](#decisions); [Answers decided on 2026-11-24, Task 62](#answers-decided-on-2026-11-24-task-62)); Task 61's colours are history too. Dark and light are one whole. It replaces the Phase 10 default, a cool grey with a teal accent, the faceplate, Coolant and Meltwater, whose colours and figures are history ([D51](#decisions)). It is used by the second and third source of [Which theme is used](#which-theme-is-used). The colours, sizes and radii below are the values; the names (Charcoal, Slate shade and so on) are for talking about them, and the code uses the tokens.

**The idea: Cool Blue Forest.** The scheme is the owner's, and the design's job is to be faithful to it and to give each colour a role. Its caption reads "soft icy grey, frosted blue, muted steel blue, deep slate blue, and dark charcoal tones of a snow-covered winter forest", and its character is low saturation in one cool family: the three blues (swatches 2, 3 and 4) sit at 206 to 210 degrees of hue and 21, 29 and 37 per cent of HSL saturation, the two greys (swatches 1 and 5) at 228 and 225 degrees and 11 and 12 per cent. Every colour of both themes is a swatch or a tint, a shade or a mix of swatches, **and none is invented**; the status colours are the only other hues. The two themes are that forest in two lights, **Dusk** (dark) and **Snowday** (light). Three rules follow, and everything below is these rules applied:
- **The greys speak, the blues build and mark.** The two greys carry what is read: the text, the muted text and, in the dark theme, the ground. The blues carry what is built and chosen: the card (a step from the charcoal towards the slate blue in the dark theme, a frosted blue in the light one) and `--accent`. So the accent is the only strongly blue ink in either theme: its chroma is 63 to 66 steps, against 12 to 15 for every text colour. It marks only what is chosen, on or focused: the selected tile's and toggle's border, an on switch's trough, a scale's filled part, a pressed gear or back icon, the focus ring and a link. It is never used for a heading, a background or decoration. This is where the palette spends its boldness, once ("Spend your boldness in one place").
- **One swatch, two roles.** Charcoal (`#2d3039`, swatch 5) is Dusk's ground and Snowday's ink; the slate blue (swatch 4) is the far end of Dusk's card and Snowday's accent; the frosted blue (swatch 2) is Snowday's ground and card. A user who flips between the two sees the same five colours trade places, not two palettes.
- **Colour means a state.** Green, yellow and red appear only as status: a dot, a banner's icon and tint, the warning text. They are always with an icon or words, never colour alone. The status green is about 60 degrees of hue from the accent and the status red about 155 degrees from it, so the accent is read neither as "good" nor as an error: an on switch and a status dot are never the same colour, and the dot always has its words.
- **The picture is the loudest thing.** The preview's and the tiles' lit dots are `--fg`: a snow-white on the slate frame in the dark theme, and the charcoal on a powder-snow frame in the light one. The accent never competes with them: it is a border and a trough, not a fill behind a picture.

**Why this isn't a generic palette, and where it comes closest to one.** Anthropic's guidance names five clusters of defaults ([Answers decided on 2026-11-19, Task 11](#answers-decided-on-2026-11-19-task-11), read again on 2026-11-22). This theme is not a warm cream with a terracotta or clay accent (every tone of it is cool, the text included); not a broadsheet; not one radius on every box (three sizes, [below](#spacing-radius-and-borders)); and has none of the template chrome: no eyebrow in capitals, no arrows, no monospace face for labels, no middle-dot meta strings of its own, no gradient wash, no soft shadow under every box, no decoration. Its dark ground is not a stand-in for black: it is the owner's charcoal, CIE lightness about 20, where `#0B0B0B` and `#111` are about 3 and 5. **It comes close to two things, and the owner's scheme is the cause of both.** The first is the second cluster, a dark ground with one light accent: the scheme is a soft blue-grey family, so a quiet dark theme with one blue mark is the brief and not an accident. The second is **Nord**, the nearest shipped Omarchy theme (`#2e3440` ground, `#3b4252` card, `#81a1c1` accent, `#d8dee9` text, read 2026-11-23): Dusk's ground is within 7 units of Nord's in every channel, because a winter forest's charcoal and Nord's polar night are one idea. What makes it this scheme's and not Nord's is modest, and said plainly: the card is a slate blue (hue 214, saturation 25 per cent) where Nord's is a greyer one (222 and 16), so the card is told from the ground by hue as well as by tone; the accent is the scheme's steel blue lifted to 5:1 on the card, lighter and bluer than Nord's; and the light theme, which Nord doesn't have, is the same five colours in daylight, not a dark theme inverted. **Depth** comes from tone alone, in three levels: the ground, the card (which is also the preview's frame and the popovers), and the controls on them, whose fills are the text colour at 4 or 5 per cent. The first two are 1.23:1 apart in the dark theme and 1.27:1 in the light one (1.24:1 in Task 61, 1.29:1 in Task 59). A gradient, a halo behind the picture, a lit edge and a shadow were considered and not chosen ([Answers decided on 2026-11-22](#answers-decided-on-2026-11-22-tasks-44-45-50-and-51)). Where the theme falls short of the guidance is said in the Task 11 answers: the type has no typeface of its own, because the app adds no package.

#### Palette

Five base tones and three status colours: the core palette of the guidance's 4 to 6 named values is the five in the first rows. Each is a hex the implementer copies; `--ctl` is `--fg` for every control. The same values, as Rust byte triples with their contrast figures and each colour's reasoning, are in [palette-winter.md](palette-winter.md). The hues are of HSL, to the degree. **The swatches** are the owner's, as given (the printed label first; where the pixels read one unit differently the label is used): 1 soft icy grey `#b3b6c2`, 2 frosted blue `#9eb1c0`, 3 muted steel blue `#6e91af`, 4 deep slate blue `#365675`, 5 dark charcoal `#2d3039` (the pixels' colour; the printed `#1d2d3f` is not its colour and is not used).

| Token | Dusk (dark): name, value, hue, source | Snowday (light): name, value, hue, source | Used for |
|---|---|---|---|
| `--bg` | Charcoal `#2d3039`, 225: swatch 5 | Hoarfrost `#c4d3e1`, 209: swatch 2 lightened by about 44 per cent white, with 1.5 times the chroma of a plain mix (Task 62; was `#d9e3ec` in Task 61 and `#d3dce3`, a 55 per cent white mix, in Task 59) | the ground's opaque tone: window, header bar, preview column, pages (painted through `--ground`) and the base of a banner's tint |
| `--surface` | Slate shade `#313f51`, 214: swatch 5 two fifths of the way to swatch 4 | Powder snow `#e6ebf1`, 213: swatch 2 with about 74 per cent white and a touch more blue (Task 62; was Fresh snow `#f9fafb` in Task 61 and `#f5f7f9` in Task 59) | cards (through `--plate`), **the tiles of the light theme (Task 61)**, the preview's frame, popovers |
| `--fg` | Snowlight `#e5ebf2`, 212: the blue-greys at 92 per cent lightness | Charcoal `#2d3039`, 225: swatch 5 | text, lit preview dots |
| `--muted` | Icy grey `#b3b6c2`, 228: swatch 1 | Ash `#4f525b`, 225: swatch 5 twenty-five per cent of the way to swatch 1 (Task 62; was `#555862`, thirty per cent) | hints, notes, reason lines (not a disabled control or a disabled heading: those are `--disabled`, since Task 63) |
| `--accent` | Steel `#94b7d6`, 208: swatch 3 lifted by 38, 38 and 39 | Deep slate `#365675`, 210: swatch 4 | the mark, as above |
| `--red` | Rowan `#e5776c`, 5 | Holly `#a9322a`, 4 (Task 62; was `#b33b32`) | an error: the status dot, the banner's icon and tint |
| `--yellow` | Larch `#e0b65c`, 41 | Resin `#704d00`, 41 (Task 62; was `#785200`) | a warning: the dot, the banner, the warning text |
| `--green` | Lichen `#79c89a`, 145 | Spruce `#2f7756`, 153 | "good": the status dot |

**Control alphas** (of `--fg`), in the order the code takes them, `[normal fill, normal border, hover fill, hover border, selected fill, pressed fill]`:

| | Dark | Light |
|---|---|---|
| normal fill, normal border | 0.05, 0.52 | 0.04, 0.62 |
| hover fill, hover border | 0.09, 0.68 | 0.07, 0.78 |
| selected fill | 0.16 | 0.14 |
| pressed fill | 0.24 | 0.20 |
| the code's array | `[0.05, 0.52, 0.09, 0.68, 0.16, 0.24]` | `[0.04, 0.62, 0.07, 0.78, 0.14, 0.20]` |
| `--unlit` (the unlit dot's alpha of `--fg`) | 0.12 | 0.12 |
| `--selection` (`--accent` at this alpha over `--bg`) | 0.25 | 0.25 |

- **What changed in Task 59:** two numbers in each theme, the normal border and the hover border (dark 0.44 and 0.60 to 0.52 and 0.68, light 0.56 and 0.74 to 0.62 and 0.78); the fills, the selected fill and the pressed fill are Meltwater's, as they were Coolant's. The scheme's grounds are mid-toned, so the same alpha draws a weaker line: at the old values the control border's ratio (row 12) would be 3.4 in the dark theme and 3.0 in the light one (on the edge), and a control's border on a card in the dark theme 3.1 (against the 3.0 it needs). With the new ones row 12 is 4.2 and 3.5, and a border on a card 3.7 and 3.9. Alphas had last changed in Task 44 (the light borders, by 0.02).
- **Task 61 changed none of the alphas.** The light ground got lighter, so row 12 rose from 3.5 to 3.6 with the same 0.62; the tile's outline left by another means (the class `own-light`), not by a softer border, which would have broken row 12 for every control.
- **Task 62 changed none of the alphas either.** The light ground is darker, so row 12 is 3.4 (3.6 before) with the same 0.62, 12.6 per cent over its need, and a control's border on a card 3.7. If a later change of the ground leaves row 12 under 3.2, the normal border alpha rises to 0.64 before the ground moves back.
- **Hover firms a border up** (0.68 over 0.52, 0.78 over 0.62): hover never weakens the border that makes the control visible. The focus fill and border are the hover ones. The selected border is `--accent` as drawn by the stylesheet, and a selected control keeps it under the pointer.
- **Focus is always visible:** every focused control also has the 2 px `--accent` outline at 2 px offset, whatever the alphas (WCAG 2.4.7). It is the steel blue in the dark theme and the deep slate blue in the light one: 6.3 and 5.0 against `--bg`, 5.1 and 6.4 against `--surface` (rows 5 and 6 of [the contrast table](#the-contrast-table); the light figures were 5.9 and 7.3 until Task 62).
- **The selection** (a selected stretch of text) is the accent at 0.25 over `--bg`, rounded: a slate in the dark theme (`#475260`) and a mid frost blue in the light one (`#a1b4c6` since Task 62; `#b0c0ce` on Task 61's ground, `#acbbc8` on Task 59's). The lowered alpha of the faceplate (0.25 against Phase 10's 0.35) stays: it keeps `--fg` text on the selection at 6.6 and 6.2 (row 15), and the selection reads as a tint of the window's own colour.
- **Computed tokens** keep their rules (`--line`, `--unlit` at `--fg` 0.12; `--banner-error` and `--banner-warn` at 0.16 and 0.14 of the status colour over `--bg`, then at the window's opacity). `--selection` is `--accent` at 0.25 over `--bg`. `--ground` and `--plate` are new ([Window opacity and corners](#window-opacity-and-corners)).
- **Text, in both lights:** the dark text is a snow-white (`#e5ebf2`, hue 212) a clear step above the muted text, which is the scheme's own icy grey, so the preview's lit dots read as lit snow on a slate frame; the light text is the scheme's charcoal itself (`#2d3039`), a neutral that keeps the accent the only blue ink. The scheme's printed navy (`#1d2d3f`, 9.2 and 11.7 on the light ground and card since Task 62, 10.1 and 13.0 before) was not used for it: beside the deep slate blue accent, at the same saturation and 1.8:1 from it, ink and links would be two navies. Nothing is warm.
- **The two lights are one identity:** the greys are 225 to 228 degrees and the blues 206 to 214 in both themes (the ground 225 and 209, the card 214 and 213, the text 212 and 225, the accent 208 and 210); the status colours keep their hues from one theme to the other and move only in lightness. The dark accent is swatch 3 lifted and the light one is swatch 4 itself, two steps of one blue ramp.

#### Type

The system font (`gtk-font-name`; no new package, rule 7). Every size is a multiple of the user's font size, so a user who sets a larger font gets a larger app, and every one is written in `em`: GTK's CSS resolves `em` in `font-size` against the inherited size ([Unverified points, answered (Phase 11a Task 11)](#unverified-points-answered-phase-11a-task-11-2026-11-19)). Five sizes in a ratio of 1.2 (0.83, 1, 1.2, 1.44 and 1.73), and two weights, 400 and 600; nothing is italic, nothing is in capitals, no letter-spacing. **One size for each level of the text, on every page and in every card** (owner, 2026-11-23: a category 14, what is in it 12, what is in that 10, whichever block it is in; and 2026-11-24: the app's title one step above the page titles, D52): the levels are the app's title in the header bar (1.73em), the page's title (1.44em), a category (1.2em), what is directly in a category (1em) and what is one level further in (0.83em); at a 12 px font that is 21, 17, 14, 12 and 10 px.

| Role (class) | Size | Weight | Colour | Used for |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `heading` | 1.44em | 600 | `--fg` | the off screen's heading ("OpenDeepCool is off"), and the not-available message's |
| `page-title` | 1.44em | 600 | `--fg` | "Display" and "Configuration" in the page bars (level 0; 1.2em until Task 55) |
| `card-heading`, `group-heading` | 1.2em | 600 | `--fg` | level 1, the categories: the cards' headings (Theme, Display, Music, Startup), the tile groups' (Load, Temperature, Power, Music) (1em until Task 55) |
| `sub-heading` | 1em | 600 | `--fg` (`--disabled` while the section is disabled) | level 2: the Theme card's "Default Theme" ([Theme card](#theme-card)), the heading of a subcategory, at the size of the card's own rows (0.83em until Task 55, which made it smaller than the rows it holds) |
| `row-title` | 1em, and 0.83em inside a subcategory | 400 | `--fg` (`--disabled` while the Default Theme is disabled) | level 2: a row's title (Use system theme, Rotation, Peak dots, Start at boot); level 3 in the Default Theme: Appearance, Window Opacity, Window Corners |
| `app-name` | 1.73em | 600 | `--fg` | level -1, the title in the header bar, "OpenDeepCool" (1em until Task 60: one step of the scale above the page title) |
| `caption` | 1em | 600 | `--fg` | the preview's caption |
| body (no class) | 1em | 400 | `--fg` | status items, tile captions, banners, buttons |
| `small` | 0.83em | 400 | `--muted` (with `muted`) | level 3: hints, notes (the Start at boot note too, since Task 55), reason lines, scale marks, a tile's flag |
| `num` | as its text | as its text | | `font-variant-numeric: tabular-nums` (as built) |

- **In the stylesheet:** `.small { font-size: 0.83em; }`, `.app-name { font-size: 1.73em; font-weight: 600; }` (Task 60; it stands where `.app-name` is, near the top of `app.css`, with `font-weight: 600` only until then), `.page-title` and `.heading { font-size: 1.44em; font-weight: 600; }`, `.card-heading` and `.group-heading { font-size: 1.2em; font-weight: 600; }`, `.sub-heading { font-weight: 600; }` and `.subcategory .row-title { font-size: 0.83em; }` (Task 55); the other roles are weight 600 alone, which `app.css` already has. `.command` takes `small`. The test `the_type_scale_has_one_size_for_each_level` (in `crates/odc-app/tests/type_scale.rs`) reads `app.css` and pins the five sizes and which class has which.
- **No compounding:** `em` in `font-size` is against the parent's size, so a class goes on the label itself, never on a box that holds another sized label. If a case of compounding is found, `rem` (GTK resolves it against the initial font size, the `gtk-font-name` size) is the fallback.
- **Hierarchy:** size steps down by one level at each depth, and weight marks a heading: a category (1.2em, 600), what is in it (1em, 400; a subcategory's heading is 1em, 600) and what is in that (0.83em), so a child is never larger than the heading that holds it (the Default Theme heading was 0.83em, smaller than its rows, until Task 55). The sub-heading's colour is `--fg` so that turning it `--disabled` is seen. The one place the mockups depart from the scale is the tile captions and flags, fixed at 12 and 11 px for their 98 px tiles; in the app they are body text.
- **A typeface of its own is a suggestion for after v1**, not a part of Task 11: a typeface with tabular figures and a squarer shape, which would give the type more character than the system font does. Any such font is a package dependency, so it goes to the owner first (rule 7).

#### Spacing, radius and borders

- **Spacing** is the existing rhythm, kept: it already follows one rule, that what belongs together is closer than what doesn't. 2 px between a title and its hint; 4 px between icon buttons; 6 px between a control's parts and under a page bar's own box (the title stands 24 px from the content, with the bar's 18 below it); 8 px between tiles; 14 px between rows and between a card's children; 18 px for a page's and the column's margins and between the cards; 24 px between a row's label and its control. A 4 px grid for all of it would move every estimate in [Size and scrolling](#size-and-scrolling) and is left for after v1.
- **The Default Theme group's spacing:** its heading stands 24 px under the line above it (the card's 14 and 10 more) and 14 px over its first row; its three rows are 14 px apart like every row ([Theme card](#theme-card)).
- **Radius,** three, by what the box is, while Window Corners is **rounded** (the default): the window **12 px** (`--radius-win`), what holds controls **10 px** (`--radius-box`: cards, the preview frame, popovers, the feedback label), controls **4 px** (`--radius`: buttons, toggles, tiles, the dropdown button, the icon buttons, the command box). Switches' troughs, scales' bars and the knobs are round, and the dots are round. While it is **sharp**, all of those are 0 except the dots. An Omarchy theme has no box, window or control radius of this theme's: its controls and boxes are 0 as before, and the compositor does the window's corners ([Window opacity and corners](#window-opacity-and-corners), the tables). The mockups draw the compositor's corners for the sample Omarchy theme as a square window.
- **Borders** are 1 px, one width everywhere; a selected tile and toggle change colour, not width, so nothing moves. In OpenDeepCool's own light theme a chosen tile and a chosen toggle in a card also draw a 1 px inset ring in `--accent` inside the border (Task 61): a shadow, so the frame is 2 px and nothing moves.
- **No gradients, no soft shadows and no transparency** other than the control alphas, the computed tokens and the window's own alpha (Task 45): the stylesheet already sets `box-shadow: none`. The one shadow is the light theme's 1 px inset ring of what is chosen, a line, not depth (Task 61), and the knobs' 1 px ring.
- **No motion in the chrome:** unchanged (`transition: none`). A theme change is a cut.

#### The contrast table

WCAG 2 relative-luminance ratios. **The figures below are the designer's hand computations for Cool Blue Forest: the dark column of 2026-11-23 (Task 59), the light column of 2026-11-24 (Task 62, for the toned-down Snowday), worked from the sRGB formula to six digits and rounded to a tenth, because the designer has no shell; the implementer computes the exact ones with `the_default_theme_has_the_contrast_the_design_states` and pins them, and a figure that differs from this table by more than a tenth, or a row under its need, goes back to the designer with its pair. In the light column six figures sit within 0.02 of a rounding edge (rows 1, 8, 10, 13, 14 and 15, row 14 by 0.012) and four more figures and one note within 0.03, and may flip by a tenth in the test, as two in the dark column may: [palette-winter.md](palette-winter.md) lists them, and a flip is only a pin to change, never a fault.** The previous tables' figures (Snowday's of Tasks 59 and 61, Meltwater's, Coolant's and the faceplate's) are gone: they belonged to the old palettes. The design chose the values to pass with margin. WCAG 2.2: 1.4.3 for text (4.5:1), 1.4.11 for the lit dot, a control's border and an accent glyph on its fill (3:1), 2.4.7 for visible focus (the outline of [the palette](#palette)). The pairs are the same as before, in their order, rows 14 and 15 included. The fill of rows 11 and 14 is the selected fill over `--surface` (row 11) and over `--bg` (row 14), as the test computes them. **All fifteen rows are computed against the opaque tones, alpha 1**; what a see-through window changes is bounded in [Window opacity and corners](#window-opacity-and-corners), section 3.

| # | Check (need) | Dark | Light |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | text on background (4.5) | 11.0 | 8.6 |
| 2 | text on surface (4.5) | 8.9 | 11.0 |
| 3 | muted text on background (4.5) | 6.5 | 5.1 |
| 4 | muted text on surface (4.5) | 5.3 | 6.5 |
| 5 | accent on background (4.5, link text, the `--bg` ring of a knob on an `--accent` bar or trough, and the chosen frame on the ground) | 6.3 | 5.0 |
| 6 | accent on surface (4.5; and the chosen frame on a card) | 5.1 | 6.4 |
| 7 | warning text (yellow) on background (4.5) | 6.9 | 5.0 |
| 8 | warning text (yellow) on surface (4.5) | 5.6 | 6.4 |
| 9 | red on background (3.0, status dots and icons) | 4.5 | 4.3 |
| 10 | green on background (3.0, status dots) | 6.6 | 3.5 |
| 11 | text on the selected fill (4.5) | 5.8 | 8.6 |
| 12 | control border on background (3.0, WCAG 1.4.11) | 4.2 | 3.4 |
| 13 | lit dot against unlit dot (3.0) | 7.8 | 7.1 |
| 14 | accent on the selected fill (3.0): the pressed gear and back icon | 4.0 | 4.0 |
| 15 | text on the selection (4.5): `--fg` on `--accent` at 0.25 over `--bg`, the selected command text | 6.6 | 6.2 |

- **The tightest pairs**, none under 11 per cent over its need in the light theme (Task 62; Task 61's was 19 and Snowday as first built 11.8) and none under 13 in the dark: in the light theme **row 7**, the warning text on the ground, 5.0 against 4.5 (11.1 per cent; hand 5.000), **row 5**, the accent on the ground, 5.0 (11.3; hand 5.010), **row 12**, the control border, 3.4 against 3.0 (12.6; hand 3.377, with the border alpha 0.62 unchanged), **row 3**, the muted text on the ground, 5.1 (13.6; hand 5.111) and **row 10**, the green dot, 3.5 against 3.0 (17.7; hand 3.532); in the dark theme **row 6**, the accent on `--surface`, 5.1 (13.4) and **row 4**, the muted text on `--surface`, 5.3 (17.7). Every other light row has 32 per cent or more. The light theme is the tighter of the two now: a ground at L\* 84 gives the accent, the hint and the warning text less room than a pale one, and the accent is the owner's swatch. The dark theme's 13.4 is tighter than Meltwater's 27: the cost of a mid-toned dark ground that is the owner's own swatch and a card one step from it.
- **Row 15's alpha 0.25** was added by Task 11 and stays: it is comfortable (6.6 and 6.2) and could rise to 0.30 if a visibly stronger selection is wanted, at no cost to the row (about 6.0 and 5.7). The light figure is 6.168 by the mockups' test (the unrounded blend) and 6.188 by the app's (the rounded `--selection`, `#a1b4c6`): both read 6.2.
- **Muted text on the selected fill** is under 4.5:1 in the dark theme (3.4, by hand 3.447), so nothing muted is drawn on a selected tile; the light theme is 5.1 there (hand 5.076, 0.026 above the rounding edge 5.05; Task 61's was 5.3, Task 59's 5.1, and `the_default_theme_has_the_contrast_the_design_states` in the mockups' tests has the light figure to change). In OpenDeepCool's light theme a chosen tile keeps its card fill, so nothing muted is drawn on a tinted tile at all; the figure is for a chosen toggle in a card.
- **The mockups' sample Omarchy theme** is made up and unchanged. The same test checks all fifteen rows against it too (each must reach its need), and its own figures stay pinned (text 11.9, muted 5.4, accent 7.2 on its background).
- **Pairs outside the table** (computed by hand from the hex values and the alphas, the dark figures on 2026-11-23 and the light ones on 2026-11-24, good to about a tenth; the test doesn't pin them):
  - **The tile flag "Reading lost"** is `--fg` on the selected fill (row 11's pair), with no warning colour; the reason line under the preview carries the warning, on `--bg` (row 7).
  - **The preview's unlit dots** sit on `--surface`, not `--bg`: lit against unlit is about 6.5 (dark) and 8.9 (light), above row 13's 3.0 (the table's 7.8 and 7.1 are on `--bg`). The tiles' unlit dots in the light theme sit on `--plate`, the same tone. In the light theme the unlit dot is `#d0d5db` on the frame `#e6ebf1`, 1.24:1 (it was `#e1e2e4` on `#f9fafb`, the same ratio a step lighter).
  - **A control's border on `--surface`** (the toggles in the cards, a switch's trough): about 3.7 (dark) and 3.7 (light), above 3.0, so row 12, computed on `--bg`, holds on a card too. It is the pair that moved the dark border alpha: at 0.44 it was 3.1.
  - **Banner text** (`--fg`) on the error tint, about 8.8 (dark) and 6.9 (light), and on the warning tint about 8.2 and 7.1; the icons on their tints: `--red` about 3.6 (dark) and 3.5 (light), `--yellow` about 5.2 (dark) and 4.1 (light), against 3.0. The light red icon is the pair that moved Holly in Task 62: with the old Holly on the darker ground it was 3.1.
  - **The chosen frame in the light theme** is `--accent` on a card, 6.4 (row 6), and on the ground, 5.0 (row 5); a tile at rest has no outline, and its edge is Powder snow against Hoarfrost, 1.27:1 (Fresh snow's was 1.24:1), which WCAG 1.4.11 doesn't ask more of for a control that its text and picture identify ([Answers decided on 2026-11-24](#answers-decided-on-2026-11-24-tasks-60-and-61)). The hover border, the control's own, is `--fg` at 0.78 over the card: about 5.8 against it. An unavailable tile's caption (`--disabled` on the well, `--fg` at 0.04 over the ground) is 2.3 since Task 63 (`--muted` made it 4.8), `--fg` on it 8.1.
  - **The disabled colour** (Task 63, D55; not a row of the table: inactive components are exempt from WCAG 1.4.3 and 1.4.11, w3.org, Understanding 1.4.3 and 1.4.11, read 2026-11-24; the figures are the designer's hand computations to six digits, and the implementer's test pins them): `--disabled` is `#798491` (relative luminance 0.226118) in the dark theme and `#80848c` (0.229852) in the light one. On `--surface`, **2.817 (dark) and 3.130 (light)**; on `--bg`, 3.466 and 2.457; `--fg` against it, 3.168 and 3.512; `--muted` on `--surface` for comparison, 5.298 and 6.511. On an unavailable tile (`--ctl-normal-fill` over `--bg`): 3.033 (dark) and 2.294 (light, the well). The hairline `--line` over `--surface`: 1.381 and 1.236 (the live unpressed border, 3.675 and 3.743). The chosen frame and an on trough are `--disabled` itself on the card. A selected fill under the label would have given the label 1.833 (dark) and 2.440 (light) instead. The made-up sample Omarchy theme (dark): `#74726a`, 2.92 on its card, 3.36 on its ground. The full table, with Omarchy and the window modes, is in [Answers decided on 2026-11-24, Task 63](#answers-decided-on-2026-11-24-task-63).
  - **The knob and its ring on an on trough:** the ring (`--bg`) against the trough (`--accent`) is row 5 (6.3 and 5.0); the knob (`--fg`) against the trough is about 1.7 in both themes (dark, where both are light, and light, where both are dark), which the ring answers by keeping the knob apart. The trough against the card is rows 5 and 6's family: `--accent` on `--surface`, 5.1 and 6.4.
  - **The accent and the status green** are about equally light in the dark theme (relative luminance 0.45 and 0.48) and 63 degrees of hue apart (57 in the light theme, where the green is lighter, 0.14 against 0.087), the nearest any status comes to the accent. Hue alone tells them apart, so the rule stands that a status is never colour alone: every status dot has its words, and the accent only ever marks a choice.
  - **The light theme's three inks** (Charcoal 0.030, Deep slate 0.087 and Ash 0.085 in relative luminance; Ash was 0.098 until Task 62): the accent is 1.7:1 from the text and 1.02:1 from the muted text, so it is told from both by chroma (63 steps against 12) and not by lightness. Row 5's 5.0 is its contrast with the ground, not with the text. Unverified in a rendered window ([the Task 59 table](#unverified-points-answered-phase-11a-task-59-2026-11-23)); Task 61's chosen frame makes the accent more visible where it matters (a 2 px line on a pale card), but not any more different in lightness from the text.
- **A pair that can't be nudged** goes back to the designer as a pair, with its ratio, and the designer changes the value, not the need.

**Where the tests change in Task 62** (2026-11-24): `PALETTES`' "default light" takes the five new colours (`background` `#c4d3e1`, `surface` `#e6ebf1`, `muted` `#4f525b`, `red` `#a9322a`, `yellow` `#704d00`; the alphas, `selected` 0.14 and `border` 0.62, don't change); the light array, in the mockups' test and in `theme.rs`'s, becomes `[8.6, 11.0, 5.1, 6.5, 5.0, 6.4, 5.0, 6.4, 4.3, 3.5, 8.6, 3.4, 7.1, 4.0, 6.2]` and the light note on the muted text on the selected fill 5.1; `theme.rs`'s `LIGHT` takes the five colours; the window test's `SNOWDAY_CARD` becomes `#e6ebf1` (`SNOWDAY_ACCENT` stays). The dark theme's figures don't change, and no `own-light` rule changes. The colours and the figures are in [palette-winter.md](palette-winter.md).

**Where the tests changed in Task 61** (2026-11-24; the colours and the light array are Task 62's now): `PALETTES`' "default light" takes the two new colours (`background` `#d9e3ec`, `surface` `#f9fafb`; the alphas, `selected` 0.14 and `border` 0.62, don't change); the table's light array becomes `[10.1, 12.6, 5.5, 6.8, 5.9, 7.3, 5.4, 6.7, 4.5, 4.1, 9.8, 3.6, 8.2, 4.6, 7.1]` and the light note on the muted text on the selected fill 5.3; `theme.rs`'s `LIGHT` takes the two colours and its comment says Task 61; `follow.rs` sets the window class `own-light`; `app.css` gets `.app-name`'s size and the seven `own-light` rules; `the_type_scale_has_one_size_for_each_level` pins `.app-name` at 1.73em; the dark theme's figures don't change. The two colours and the figures are in [palette-winter.md](palette-winter.md).

**Where the tests changed before** (Task 44; the values and figures redone in Tasks 54 and 59): `PALETTES` in `tools/mockups/tests/mockups.rs` takes the values of the palette tables above for "default dark" and "default light" (the sample's stay; **the normal and hover border alphas of both themes changed in Task 59, so `border` is 0.52 and 0.62, and `selected` 0.16 and 0.14 stays**), its table pins the 15 figures the test computes, and the one-decimal notes on the muted text on the selected fill become the computed figures: the dark theme's (3.6 until Task 59) is about 3.4 (hand 3.447, so the exact figure may read 3.5) and stays under 4.5, and the light theme's (5.3) is about 5.1; `crates/odc-app/src/theme.rs` takes `DARK` and `LIGHT` (the eight colours of each and **two of the six alphas**, the normal border and the hover border; the selection alpha 0.25 and the radii of [Window opacity and corners](#window-opacity-and-corners) stay) and the 15 figures of `the_default_theme_has_the_contrast_the_design_states`, and the doc comments of both constants name Cool Blue Forest; `the_palettes_are_the_mockups_own` reads `app.css`'s two blocks, which keep the same custom-property names (`app.css` already has Cool Blue Forest's values, and the mockups are regenerated). The Glassy bounds table of [Window opacity and corners](#window-opacity-and-corners), section 3, is not pinned by a test. The exact `Palette` literals and the figures are in [palette-winter.md](palette-winter.md).

#### The Theme card's mockups

The card is on the Configuration page, so every mockup that shows that page (configuration, configuration-gpu-list, one-gpu, boot-partly, boot-unknown) shows it in state (a), with an Omarchy theme found and followed (the sample Omarchy theme, dark: the Default Theme group disabled, in the grey disabled look of D55, Appearance on Dark, Window Opacity on Opaque and Window Corners on Rounded). Three pages show other theme inputs, and since Task 50 a fourth, `configuration-theme-glassy.html`, shows the window's own look. Their data keys (all optional; the defaults give the earlier pages' state) are `omarchyTheme`, `colorScheme`, `useSystemTheme` and `darkTheme`, and, since Tasks 44 to 51, `windowOpacity`, `windowCorners` and `omarchyWindowOpacity` ([mockups-src/app.js](mockups-src/app.js), its header):

| File | Title | Data | Shows |
|---|---|---|---|
| `configuration-theme-system.html` | Configuration, the system's light preference | `omarchyTheme` false, `colorScheme` "light" | No Omarchy theme, and the system prefers light, so the window opens in OpenDeepCool's light theme, opaque and rounded. The switch is on, the Default Theme group is disabled (grey, Appearance with Light framed, Window Opacity on Opaque, Window Corners on Rounded), and the line says "Using OpenDeepCool's light theme, as your desktop prefers." Turn the switch off in the mockup: the group comes alive and the theme follows `DarkTheme` |
| `configuration-theme-own.html` | Configuration, OpenDeepCool's own theme | `useSystemTheme` false, `darkTheme` false | An Omarchy theme is found but isn't followed: the switch is off and sensitive, the whole group is sensitive with Light pressed, and the window is in OpenDeepCool's light theme, opaque, with rounded corners. Click Dark, and the window turns dark; click Transparent, Glassy, Sharp or Rounded, and the window changes at once |
| `configuration-theme-not-found.html` | Configuration, no system theme found | `omarchyTheme` false, `colorScheme` "unsupported" | The switch is off and disabled (a hairline trough and a grey knob), the warning line says "OpenDeepCool can't find the system's theme and is using its own.", and the Default Theme group is sensitive with Dark pressed, in OpenDeepCool's dark theme |
| `configuration-theme-glassy.html` (**new**) | Configuration, a glassy window | `useSystemTheme` false, `windowOpacity` "glassy", `windowCorners` "sharp", `omarchyWindowOpacity` "glassy" | OpenDeepCool's own dark theme with Glassy and Sharp chosen: the window is see-through at 0.75 over a striped backdrop (mockup chrome), blurred, with square corners on the window, the cards, the controls and the switches. Turn Use system theme on: the group turns disabled (grey), and the window takes the sample Omarchy theme and Omarchy's mode, which this page sets to glassy too, so it stays glassy while the corners are the sample's. Click Opaque or Rounded to see the other settings |

The refused change (d) has no page of its own: it is the overlay label every refusal uses.

## Keyboard
